# ALM Release 7 V1c step 2: the setup burst, the leader-ACK path and the harness at scale

V1c step 1 (#652) delivered bounded lane service and the consumer-proof workload; the original
capture-off 15-agent acceptance still fails at setup (HTTP timeouts at the 5,000 ms command limit
behind a 35 s AppInbox queue) and in traffic (84 shots arrive at the director, 15–71 leader-ACK
observers time out inside the 30 s window). This proposal records the static performance audit of
2026-10-14, the GitHub Free review, the decisions D194 to D204 and the split of step 2 into two
slices. It implements the maintainer's standing constraints in
[the V1c plan](../../plans/active/alm-v1c-scale-evidence-implementation-plan.md): both the Rallar
production code and the black-box harness are slow at scale, the queuebox engine is the
event-handling core and must stay quick and fault tolerant, the recipes run locally first, and no
legacy, duplication or migration code is added.

## 1. Static audit

Five read-only audits ran against `main` at `2d5ffe35f` (static analysis as hypothesis generation
under the `performance-analysis` skill; every row carries a confidence label; nothing below was
measured unless it says so). The hosted API is one `prod-in-memory` Deno process with in-process
PGlite: SQL runs on the handler's JavaScript thread under an exclusive transaction lock, so every
statement, poll and serialization is event-loop time, and more worker concurrency adds no CPU there.

### 1.1 Queuebox engine and the server AppInbox

| Id  | Confidence         | Where                                                                                                                                                                 | What it costs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Fix                                                                                                                                        |
| --- | ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| F1  | Proven (mechanism) | `packages/shared/services/inbox-outbox-engine.ts:351-366`, `packages/shared/resilience/ComputeAsyncTask.ts:258-294`, `rallar-middleware-queue-registration.ts:99-103` | A pass that finds the single AppInbox slot busy counts as idle and doubles the delay 100→3,000 ms; nothing wakes the engine when the batch settles, so after a 3.7 s handler the next claim is ≈2.5 s late. Plausible owner of the 4.4 s of the failed JOIN's 35.2 s queue age not spent in handlers. | Wake the engine from the registration runnable after a batch that claimed work (inbox and outbox).                                         |
| F2  | Proven             | `create-default-resource-inbox-dequeuer.ts:332-341`, `p-sql-queue-box.ts:304-339`                                                                                     | After the handler's transaction already marked the entry COMPLETED, the dequeuer opens a release transaction: BEGIN, an UPDATE binding the full payload twice that matches 0 rows, `SELECT *`, COMMIT — 4 of ≈12 queue statements per entry.                                                          | Return the handler-finalized entry as a typed success and skip the release, mirroring the failure path's `ResourceInboxHandlerEntryError`. |
| F3  | Proven (schedule)  | `app-inbox-result-waiter.ts:184-311`, `defaults-config.json:119-126`                                                                                                  | Each waiting request polls status at 250, 500, then every 1,000 ms for up to 30 s: ≈15/30/50 SELECTs per second on the serialized database during the burst, although the same-process completion notification already exists.                                                                        | Config: raise `completionWait.maxRetryIntervalMs` in `prod-in-memory`; the poll stays as the fallback.                                     |
| F4  | Proven             | `rallar-middleware-queue-registration.ts:93`                                                                                                                          | `maxConcurrency: () => 1`; the `rateAdjuster` is updated and never read. Per-principal WS authorizations block unrelated JOINs in one FIFO lane. Large on Postgres, ≈0 on hosted PGlite.                                                                                                              | Configurable concurrency, default 1, read through `ResourceInboxResilience` (2b, Postgres evidence).                                       |
| F5  | Proven             | `app-inbox-queue-entry-writer.ts:67`, `queue-box-pub-sub-bridge.ts:398`                                                                                               | Other servers are never woken for AppInbox enqueues; idle servers notice within ≤3.6 s.                                                                                                                                                                                                               | AppInbox work notice on the pub/sub bridge (after F4).                                                                                     |
| F6  | Proven             | `create-default-resource-inbox-dequeuer.ts:225-246`, `resource-inbox-resilience.ts:43`                                                                                | A crashed, lease-lost or wedged claim is recovered only after 5 min (+≤60 s discovery) against a 30 s client wait. A stale finisher already fails the attempt CAS, so a shorter lease is safe.                                                                                                        | An AppInbox reservation lease of 60 s for the TIMEOUT lane; finalization recovery stays at 5 min.                                          |
| F7  | Proven             | `ComputeAsyncTask.ts:12`, `inbox-outbox-engine.ts:296-313`                                                                                                            | A handler await that never settles holds the only slot forever; no runnable deadline, no statement timeout.                                                                                                                                                                                           | Postgres `statement_timeout`; a runnable deadline that abandons the slot (2b).                                                             |
| F8  | Proven             | `queue-box-utilities.ts:40-65`, `circuit-breaker.ts:155-176`, `inbox-outbox-engine.ts:351-355`                                                                        | Typed retryable conflicts count as breaker failures (11 in 10 s halts all AppInbox for 10 s); while open, `isWork` still reports work so the engine re-runs every 100 ms with 3 SQL and a log line per pass.                                                                                          | Exclude typed retryable conflicts from `failure()`; breaker-open reports no work and `wakeAt` the reopen time (the ALM lanes' pattern).    |
| F9  | Proven             | `ComputeAsyncTask.ts:258-262`, `inbox-outbox-engine.ts:264-290`                                                                                                       | One task's `isWork` error aborts the whole pass for every task; 11 in a minute open the engine-wide breaker.                                                                                                                                                                                          | Catch per task and report through `reportTaskFailure`.                                                                                     |
| F10 | Proven             | `dequeue-controller.ts:274-282, 310-325, 357-368`                                                                                                                     | A reserve or release error sleeps 1–16 s per lane inside the slot, not abortable.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Return from the lane and `wakeAt(now + backoff)`.                                                                                          |
| F11 | Proven             | `resource-inbox-results-repository.ts:56-65`, `create-api-v1-runtime.ts:291-296`                                                                                      | `resource_inbox_results` rows expire but are never deleted; in PGlite they live in the WASM heap for the process lifetime.                                                                                                                                                                            | Add the results repository to the existing expiry eviction loop.                                                                           |
| F12 | Strong suspicion   | `computed-data-validation.ts:54-160`, `client-state-inbox-computation.ts:274-291`                                                                                     | Validation walks the complete result with eagerly built path strings and double descriptor reads; see §1.2 for the handler-level multiplier.                                                                                                                                                          | Profile at 15/30/50 cardinality before changing (2b).                                                                                      |

Checked with no finding: completion-subscription timers, the engine's single timer, duplicate
delivery (claim is `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` + attempt-guarded finish), retry exhaustion (20 attempts).

### 1.2 Session-setup handlers (`CLIENT_AUTHORISED_WS_CONNECT`, group ensure/join)

| Id | Confidence                                  | Where                                                                                                             | What it costs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Fix                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -- | ------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| W1 | Strong suspicion                            | `client-snapshot-provenance.ts:48-52,109`, `protocol/canonical-json.ts:8-14`                                      | Provenance awaits `crypto.subtle.digest` once per outbox row, sequentially, and validation recomputes it: ≈6 event-loop yields per handler under a host measured 99.7 % busy with 33.5 s run-queue wait. Reads and writes await ≈30 times yet took 10 % of handler time; the digest yields are the outlier for the 79 % spent in compute + validate. | A synchronous canonical SHA-256 (`node:crypto` `createHash`, available in Deno and Node); every caller renamed, no alias; bytes unchanged; compute and validate become synchronous. |
| W2 | Proven                                      | `compute-client-mutation-result.ts:62-64`, `client-snapshot-provenance.ts:33-47,65`                               | The state-sync pages are built four times per handler (compute, provenance self-check, and both again in validation), each build ≥6 full JSON passes per row.                                                                                                                                                                                        | Build provenance from the rows compute already produced.                                                                                                                            |
| W3 | Proven                                      | `client-state-inbox-computation.ts:277-278`, `assert-client-mutation.ts:63`, `assert-client-persistence.ts:69-73` | Validation projects the same data three times: the whole operation, the mutation sub-tree again, then persistence recomputed and compared a third time.                                                                                                                                                                                              | One complete recompute and one projection; keep the O(1) identity and guard invariants.                                                                                             |
| W4 | Proven (shape), magnitude needs measurement | `read-group-state-snapshots-for-principal.ts:71,79,99-105`, `read-client-mutation.ts:55-84`                       | Every connect scans every member row in the scope with a suffix filter, twice, then loads every co-member group and every co-group principal's snapshot to fill `principalAudienceSessionIds`: O(N) rows per connect, O(N²) per burst; ≈16 reads and 13 writes per connect, serial.                                                                  | Make the second pass an exact-key re-read (owner ruling on the linearization point); measure the scope size first.                                                                  |
| W5 | Proven                                      | `group-state-inbox-handler.ts:209-216`, `group-state-inbox-result.ts:67-79,168-198`                               | Each group join re-reads and encodes the full group snapshot into its completion row and recomputes it in validation: O(M) per join, O(M²) bytes per burst.                                                                                                                                                                                          | Measure, then decide whether the completion row needs the full snapshot.                                                                                                            |
| W6 | Needs measurement                           | `auth/mutation/read-auth-mutation-attempt.ts:135`, `apps/api-v1/src/routes/ws-routes.ts:99-112`                   | Agent-ticket sessions share the issuing operator's `clientId` and the WS route sets no principal or client instance; if hosted agents connect on ticket sessions, each connect emits 1+N full-snapshot rows and N+2 digests.                                                                                                                         | Capture live sessions per principal in one hosted 15-agent run; an identity decision is a product ruling.                                                                           |

### 1.3 Browser ALM: the leader-ACK path

| Id | Confidence                    | Where                                                                                                                                                 | What it costs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Fix                                                                                                          |
| -- | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| B1 | Proven                        | `resolve-al-owned-child-peer-ids.ts:24-38`, `al-policy.ts:595-608`, `compute-al-inbound-admission.ts:251-272`, `transition-al-pending-ack.ts:104-108` | A `group-leader` send is frozen to the director, yet every node forwards it to all overlay neighbours and the director defers its own ACK until every forwarded child subtree completes. One silently dropped copy below the director (`no-targets` after a topology change, `unauthorized`/`edge-unavailable` without NACK) blocks that shot's ACK for good. The existing multihop test places the director only at a leaf. | The logical recipient ACKs itself immediately with status `accepted` beside the deferred subtree row (D202). |
| B2 | Proven                        | `al-inbound-effect-intent.ts:204-218`, `validate-al-outbound-control-admission.ts:58-70`                                                              | Every node's ACK is relayed hop by hop to the origin, which refuses all but the director's: ≈150–250 control sends per shot at 50 nodes, each spending a token of the per-session 20/s RTC limiter that data forwards bypass.                                                                                                                                                                                                | Relays forward only ACKs whose logical recipient is in the frozen audience.                                  |
| B3 | Proven; needs >1 API instance | `ws-queue-box-server-ack-relay.ts:18-19,119-133`, `ws-queue-box-server-service.ts:456-458`                                                            | On the WS fallback leg a director's relayed ACKs are capped at 60 per 60 s; over budget or after a failed NOTIFY they are dropped with no NACK.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Budget per held aggregate; a failed NOTIFY returns `Left`.                                                   |
| B4 | Proven                        | `normalize-al-qos-policy.ts:226,303-309`, `al-outbound-repair-admission.ts:201-238`, `compute-al-delivery-lifecycle.ts:34`                            | 2 s ACK timeout × 3 attempts: WS takes over only after ≈8 s, each retry re-floods the tree, and a late RTC ACK is discarded after the hand-over; shots 5 and 6 have 10 s and 5 s budgets.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Rare once B1 and B2 land; measured, not changed.                                                             |
| B5 | Proven                        | `read-al-inbound-work-selection.ts:422-446`, `in-memory-queue-box.ts:245-265,411-421`, `al-admission-backend.ts:150-172`                              | `send-control` ranks last in each 16-claim batch; `finalizeExhausted` copies and filters the whole in-memory queue every batch because COMPLETED rows stay for 60 s; the memory backend's `list(prefix)` decodes the whole map.                                                                                                                                                                                              | Rank `send-control` first; prune COMPLETED rows on release; prefix-indexed listing.                          |
| B6 | Proven                        | `al-outbound-receipt-admission.ts:61-72`, `ws-queue-box-client-service.ts:275-278`                                                                    | A server receipt is dropped after three commit conflicts on the WS leg; the handle stays pending to its deadline.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Retain the conflicting receipt as pending-control work, as `retainPendingControl` already does.              |

Premise corrections from the audit: volatile shots and their ACKs never touch IndexedDB (the pages
that wedged at 280 admissions carried `local-inbox` traffic); the "≥34/s refuses every own send"
figure predates the D189 own-share rule; ACK controls bypass the volatile budget.

### 1.4 Black-box harness

| Id | Confidence          | Where                                                                                                                    | What it costs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Fix                                                                                                                                                     |
| -- | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| H1 | Proven (complexity) | `control-agent-sockets.ts:94-97`, `control-service.ts:469,1099-1131`, `group-assertions-evidence.ts:122-135,151-172`     | Every heartbeat, result, registration or barrier arrival re-runs the full distributed-run rollup once per agent: 231 group assertions × N rows, each row filtering all N evidences and re-flattening the agent's result tree — O(A·N³) per message, ≈29 M row steps for one 50-agent barrier release, on the same host as the hosted API.         | One rollup per affecting message with evidence indexed by agent.                                                                                        |
| H2 | Proven              | `runtime/event-history.ts:80`, `control-client.ts:704`, `to-control-agent-report.ts:69,74`, `wait/wait-for-event.ts:142` | Each runtime event copies the whole event array and is rescanned twice; every live wait rescans everything (a player carries up to 11 absent waits in the window). The #651 pacing and HMR-off fixes landed only in `apps/rallar-black-box`; the all-local lane serves `rallar-black-box-headless`, which rebuilds its DOM about twice per event. | Event cursor, report projection only while shown, incremental wait matching; the headless page gets the paced render and `hmr: false` under full-stack. |
| H3 | Proven              | `create-alm-scale-workload-commands.ts:152-158`, `create-alm-scale-arrival-commands.ts:6-50`                             | Shot 6 is sent at ≈25 s because of chained absent-wait offsets; the last ≈5 s must hold the ACKs, the barrier, 48 player verification commands and 252/522/882 director arrival commands, each paying H2.                                                                                                                                         | Measured first; moving verification out of the window is a maintainer ruling (D204).                                                                    |
| H4 | Strong suspicion    | `control-client.ts:662-670`, `RALLAR_BLACK_BOX_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`                                                        | A result frame above the 2 MB control limit is closed with 1009 and resent on every reconnect; the dropped event still counts as published.                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Oversize-frame diagnostic, gap record, bounded replay.                                                                                                  |
| H5 | Proven              | `control-runtime-retention.ts:25-28`, `control-snapshot-persistence.ts:27,88-97`                                         | The server spreads every retained array on every message even with unbounded retention, and rewrites a pretty-printed snapshot every 100 ms.                                                                                                                                                                                                      | No copy when retention is unbounded; compact, change-driven snapshot persistence.                                                                       |

Other harness facts: `messages.observe` is event-driven; `messages.received` polls every 10 ms with
a full scan; barrier timeouts resolve lazily on the next heartbeat; one failed page at startup kills
the whole worker and discards its logs; `almDiagnostics: true` streams events no assertion reads.

### 1.5 Where the time goes (hypothesis chains to confirm by measurement)

- **Setup burst (35 s queue age, HTTP 503 at 5 s):** serial handlers (F4) × per-handler cost
  (W1–W3, ≈3.7 s hosted) + idle gaps between batches (F1) + a serialized database shared with
  O(sessions) status polls (F3) and 4 redundant statements per entry (F2), on a host also running
  the control server's O(A·N³) rollups (H1).
- **Local baseline (this Mac, 2026-10-14, all-local lifecycle, memory API, 15 agents):** every page
  sets up and reaches traffic; the run fails in the players' 30 s windows with 240
  `ALM_DELIVERY_STATE_TIMEOUT` observations (the shot ACK observers) and 234 parallel timeouts, while
  the harness streams 55,954 `rtc-diagnostic` events in 54 s. Locally the setup burst passes and
  the ACK path is the blocker; hosted, the setup burst fails first. Step 2a therefore takes the ACK
  path and the harness, step 2b the server engine and the handler cost.
- **Match window (ACK observers time out although shots arrive):** B1 (the director waits for the
  tree) amplified by B2 (ACK flood on the shared limiter) and B4 (8 s to fallback, retries re-flood),
  with the harness leaving ≈5 s for the last shot and its verification (H3) on pages doing O(history)
  work per event (H2).

### 1.6 Measured on the step 2a head (local, this Mac)

| Cohort | Result                                    | Evidence                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------ | ----------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 15     | passed, 15/15 recipes, 231/231 assertions | 37,022 events (55,998 before the slice)                                                                                                                        |
| 30     | passed, 30/30 recipes, 231/231 assertions | first local 30-agent pass; ACK latency p50 901 ms, p90 1.8 s, max 2.5 s; every attempt over RTC                                                                |
| 50     | failed in the match window                | all 50 set up; 18 distinct shot-6 timeouts at 3.8–4.5 s after send, all `transport-accepted`; 22 shots-complete barrier timeouts then fail every player window |

What the 50-agent artifacts show (`scratchpad` diagnosis, numbers from `observation/events.jsonl`
and `results.jsonl`): the accepted tree put the director as a **leaf at depth 7** behind one
degree-5 relay (at 30 the director was the root with four children, depth 4), and ACK latency grows
with depth — p50 152 ms at depth 2, 1.35 s at 3, 2.64 s at 4, 4.0 s at 5, 4.6 s at 6, 5.2 s at 7 —
so the last shot's ACK from deep players cannot fit the window's remaining ≈5 s; attempts per
acknowledged shot rose from 1.9 to 3.4 (max 15), congestion deferrals from 0 to 615 (480 on one
hub relay) and ACK submissions dropped before sending from 0 to 182, while the director stayed at
or under 20 ACK submissions per second and 164/1,000 admissions; only 7 shots fell back to WS; the
per-session 20/s limiter, the breaker and the RTC data-channel queue emit no diagnostics, so they
are unobserved. Harness load rose to ≈9,000 events per second (180 per agent, 94 % ALM
diagnostics; 3,400 at 30), 75 % of events reached the control server more than 1 s late (up to
37 s), and 13 agents' recipe results never arrived, although the pages' own 5 s stats cadence and
trivial command latencies stayed on time. Ranked causes for step 2b: (1) director placement and
tree depth (topology planning), (2) per-hop ALM cost and retry churn at hub relays, (3) the harness
diagnostic volume making the evidence lossy, (4) unobserved limiter/breaker/queue state needing a
diagnostic before it can be ruled in or out.

### 1.7 Measured on the hosted plane during step 2b

| Head                                              | Hosted 15 (manifest 19)     | What the artifacts show                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `bf29cf846` (engine + handler cost)               | failed at setup             | no more 503s; 8 player connects hit the recipe command timeout in the room-join phase and the director timed out on RTC readiness; every agent signed in as the one shared operator user, so each WebSocket connect fanned out 1+N snapshot pages for that principal (§1.2 W6)                                    |
| `cbe594790` (one user per agent, `controller-NN`) | failed at the first command | the fan-out is gone; the 15 registrations (`register: 'if-needed'`, PBKDF2-SHA256 at 120,000 iterations) serialize on the AppInbox at ≈350 ms each inside the first HTTP command's 5 s allowance, so the agents at the back of the queue are cancelled (one registered in 4.9 s and passed, the next was cut off) |

| `69d75baef` (sign-in step, materializer) | failed at RTC readiness | every agent signs in (3.9–4.7 s, almost all registration) and passes its group requests under 5 s; WebSocket connect still takes 13–24 s and room-join 14–21 s on the serial AppInbox, so the readiness waits start 45–69 s in; the topology is planned on time and v15 is accepted by all 15 within 5.7 s of the last join; then, as 15 Chromium agents set up 22 RTC edges on the one VM that also runs the API, the control server and Caddy, the WebSocket signalling relays at ≈11 s per hop, every control socket drops for ≈30 s (502s on the operator's polls) and the readiness deadlines pass before the first answers land (three pairs did open, ICE in under 1 s); on reconnect the control server redispatches unfinished commands and four agents started a second recipe run ("a barrier id is single-use") — a harness fault-tolerance defect for step 2c |

The sign-in therefore becomes an explicit first recipe step under the 45 s setup allowance (an
`http.request` to an authenticated read; the group requests keep their 5 s allowance), which is
the design's phase-budget rule applied to authentication. The registration cost itself is the
password derivation's security parameter and stays; a cohort of 50 then signs in within ≈18 s.

| `9884c5078` (GitHub Free `hetzner-control`, browsers on GitHub runners) | **passed**, 15/15, 231/231, 76.7 s | run 38662616218: zero failed, missing or stale agents; the first hosted 15-agent pass of the V1c workload |

The hosted 15-agent limit is now the co-located VM: planning and ICE are fine, the shared host
stalls both server processes once the browsers start. The GitHub Free workflow's `hetzner-control`
mode (§2) places the browsers on GitHub runners and leaves the VM to the API and the control
server; it is the hosted path for 15/30/50 until the VM is sized for co-located agents. The
deciding telemetry for the stall (host CPU and steal, the api-v1 and control-server unit logs,
Caddy upstream errors between 00:33:48 and 00:34:20Z on run 38660427046) is not in the artifacts.

### 1.8 Measured on the step 2b head (local, this Mac)

| Cohort | Result                                                  | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 15     | passed, 231/231                                         | 6,187 events (37,022 on the step 2a head)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 30     | passed, 231/231                                         | 12,889 events; shot-ACK latency p50 399 ms, p90 1.27 s                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 50     | all 294 shots acknowledged; the director's window fails | 24,553 events (272,925 before); the director is the root of a 4-deep tree (degree 5); shot-ACK p50 1.29 s, p90 2.07 s, by depth 49 ms / 542 ms / 1.54 s / 1.79 s at depths 1–4; attempts per acknowledged shot equal the sender's degree; `rateLimited` 4,793 (1,228 at 30, 72 at 15) on every agent; the start event's 49 confirmations took 1.51 s; the shots-complete barrier resolved 1.15 s after its last arrival (0.02 s at 30); the end event was sent at 29.62 s and its ACK was cut off at 30.01 s with every player having received it |

The remaining 50-agent gap is the window budget, not a lost message: the 25 s shot schedule, the
depth tail, the barrier and the 49-recipient end ACK sum to ≈31 s. The window stays (D204); step 2c
takes the acknowledgement tail (its stepped completion at ≈1.25 s then every 0.5 s points at a retry
or acknowledgement timer; the limiter's wait is unmeasured) and the control plane's barrier latency
(parent result frames re-embed their children's results, ≈58 MB of 159 MB).

Offer→answer relay latency per cohort (p50, from `rallar.browser.rtc.signaling_diagnostics`): local 15/30/50 = 173/447/926 ms; hosted 15/30 = 4,261/7,072 ms. The hosted API is the single-lane `prod-in-memory` profile, so every signal relay, join and status read waits behind the same serial queue; the cost grows with the cohort on both planes and is ≈8–15× higher hosted.

| Cohort    | Result                                                                                                                     | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

### 1.14 Measured on the formation tail 2 head (local, this Mac, memory API; hosted through GitHub Free `hetzner-control` with the API on Postgres)

Four read-only surveys of `fadcd3e93` (main after #675; labels as in §1.1) located the remaining setup waits at 50:

- **The NEW lane ran in rounds** (proven from code). `dequeueLane` reserved up to K heads and `computeDequeueBatch`
  awaited `Promise.all` over the whole reservation before one release and the next reservation
  (`dequeue-batch.ts:40-56`, `dequeue-controller.ts:283-314`), so at K = 4 a round lasted as long as its slowest
  handler while the freed slots idled; the D301 exclusion chain lived inside one batch call and could not order rows
  across reservations.
- **The engine absorbed a wake** (proven by a RED test, 0.090 s on the fake clock). A wake that arrives while only a
  task's runnable holds its single slot schedules a pass at 0 that finds the slot busy, counts as idle and sets the
  0.100 s ± 20 % backoff; when the runnable ends without a wake of its own, the next pass waits that backoff out. The
  main head's local 50 with phase timing spent 4.561 s of a 6.677 s setup window idle while the next pass's rows
  already waited, with handlers of 0.004–0.030 s; rebuilt from the handler records, the largest inbox-only gaps are
  0.211–0.396 s, each after a pass that claimed 8 rows. The absorbed wake is located; its share of those gaps is not
  (strong suspicion; the pass-boundary line of D308 measures it, zero-claim passes included).
- **The authorized-WS connect wrote 15 statements on the first connect and 14 on the next** (counter scope; the
  survey's static count was 13), serially in one transaction: ticket, lifecycle, four runtime rows, the event, three
  WS_OUTBOX pages, two provenance proofs and two completion statements. The group path already writes its runtime
  rows as one guarded-batch statement.
- **Its audience-group read probed G groups one at a time** through the group snapshot cache: G + 4 statements, 25
  at G = 21 (0.503/0.923 s median/p95 in the #675 capture).
- **The co-member read carried never-expiring rows.** It transferred P principals, P·I client-instance rows and the
  sessions, while its only consumer (`computePrincipalStateSyncAudience`) uses the active sessions; instance rows
  never expire, so I grows with every hosted run.
- **The `prefix-suffix` member read scanned the workspace.** It ranged over every member row of the scope and
  filtered by `right()`, so the reversed-key index of D283 was unusable.
- **A held inbound offer waited out its retry stage.** An offer refused `retry/stage-layout-mismatch` is re-dued by
  the inbox retry schedule (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 s after the millisecond steps); layout adoption started outbound dials but
  never re-dued the retained row, and the observed retry→application maxima of 3.5–7.8 s match the 1/2/4 s stages.
- **The harness polled and relayed.** The audience wait read `GET …/groups/{id}` every 0.200 s per agent (up to
  ≈ 250 reads/s at 50), every routine browser diagnostic travelled as its own control frame, and the 1.3 MB runtime
  chunk was imported at the first command.

statements, and a stale principal revision or a spent ticket rolls back at statement 1 as a typed conflict. The
audience-group read over 21 unchanged groups is 5 statements (25): two selections before, one version probe, two

- **The per-pass context cap and the pass barrier** (proven). From 20.3 to 63.0 s, 26 back-to-back passes each claim
  their 8-context cap with 36–48 rows still waiting, and a pass claims nothing new until it drains (D306 pipelines

  window, the outbound admission commit 1.483/4.130 s p50/p95.

- **The event loop** (needs measurement): the per-second maximum lag is 144.5 ms median and 578 ms at most in the

- **The PBKDF2 admission** (strong suspicion): a login spends 6.254 s p50 before its inbox row exists, against a
  0.305 s inbox wait.
- **Persistent-store scans** (proven, minor inside the window): the sign-in read's group scan takes 0.755 s p50.

### 1.15 Measured on the formation tail 3 head (local, this Mac, memory API; hosted through GitHub Free `hetzner-control` with the API on Postgres)

Three read-only surveys of `c0e3b6e15` (main after #687) asked why the AppInbox lane stays the admission floor at 50.
They started from the timing-on hosted 50 of §1.14 and a model of the lane's code paths. Model figures are labelled
as such; they are not measurements.

- **A pass serves each context once and then drains** (proven from code). A pass serves a context's head at most
  once, stops after `NEW_CONTEXTS_PER_PASS` = 8 claims and waits for every claimed row. A player's next command
  therefore waits for the end of the pass, not only for a slot. Hosted: 26 back-to-back passes each claim 8 with
  36–48 rows still waiting.
- **Chained group rows hold places they cannot use** (proven from code). The lane holds at most
  `max(maxToReserve, K)` = 4 unreleased rows and refills only when fewer than K are unreleased. A row waiting behind
  its exclusion predecessor counts as unreleased but holds no slot. Hosted symptom: 12.8 s of in-pass idleness with
  only group-chained rows waiting.
- **Slot admission is first in, first out** (model). A ready group row queues behind WS connects of about 1 s each.
  Admitting it first is worth about 3 s at 50 in the model.
- **The recovery lanes wait for the NEW lane** (proven from code). RETRY, FAIRNESS and TIMEOUT run only after NEW
  drains, so a raised cap makes a conflicted join wait a longer pass.
- **Model at 50, last arrival / player p50:** today 43.8 / 38.0 s; a cap of 32 alone 39.7 / 34.4 s, with RETRY
  waiting up to about 10 s per pass; a persistent flight with a hold of 16 and keyed-first admission 34.4 / 28.0 s.
- **Presence sits on the group chain by key only** (proven from code). A join compares-and-sets the group row and
  bumps its versions. Presence connect, heartbeat and disconnect guard their own session row and the principal's
  admission row, never the group row. Of the 24.187 s of serial GROUP_* handler time at 50, presence connects are
  8.552 s (35 %) and joins 13.133 s.
- **A join reads in three statements over two sequential steps** (proven from code). The exact read and the result
  snapshot are separate statements, and a summary that moves between them raises the result-read-conflict retry.
  That the five hosted join retries are this class is a strong suspicion.
- **The pool is five connections on the hosted lane** (proven from code). With the listener the API holds seven
  Postgres connections, and no environment variable can change the pool. A transaction holds its connection from
  `BEGIN` to `COMMIT`, so the 7.1 ms counter-scope wait per statement is not pool wait. Formation demand above five
  is a strong suspicion.

What landed (D321–D324, D328), each RED first at its owner:

- **A persistent NEW flight** (D321, D322). The AppInbox reader keeps one flight across passes. A pass tops it up to
  H = 16 held rows and returns. A keyed row released by a same-key predecessor takes the freed slot first (D328).
  Contexts with a row in flight are excluded from every reservation lane, and the recovery lanes share the flight.
- **Presence off the group chain** (D323). Presence commands are keyed per session; joins and the group's own
  commands keep a group key.
- **One join read** (D324). A join reads its policy, decision rows and result snapshot in one batch statement.
- **The pool facts** (D325). The startup summary names the pool, K and H, and the counter scope measures the wait
  before a transaction begins.

Postgres evidence (real `PSqlQueueBox`, the AppInbox reader at K = 4 and H = 16). The walls are from `ffafeb51a`;
every test passes on `e6f4d1b63`:

| Test                                                     | Result                                                                           |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 170 rows: 50 contexts × 3, plus 20 under one key         | Draining lane 956–1,074 ms in 22 passes; flight 528–543 ms. Every row attempt 1  |
| One instance                                             | No context ever holds two RESERVED rows; spans under one key never overlap       |
| Two readers on one store                                 | Contexts with two RESERVED rows across readers: 14 draining, 40–50 flight (D326) |
| Two presence connects against a join held in its handler | Both connects commit while the join waits; attempts 1; on both lane modes        |
| Join read, counter scope                                 | 4 → 2 statements, 3 → 2 round trips; presence connect 3 → 2 round trips          |

Verdict (D330): the whole-formation benefit is verified. Both adjacent primary contrasts favour the head, and the
symmetric −9.361 s (60.068 → 50.707 s) is larger than the +6.024 s baseline drift. The gain lies in group
created→connected, mainly RTC readiness and room join, each favourable in both contrasts. SDK connect does not
improve (−2.634 and +4.247 s; symmetric +0.807 s). The end of formation is flat and does not meet the rule
(−0.053 and +0.029 s), because main already carries #687's end-of-formation repair and all four legs sit at
0.31–0.39 s. Recipe start→group created is slower on both B legs (+3.6 s), before the primary window. All legs drift
upward on the growing store (A1 57.1 → A4 63.1 s, B2 44.7 → B3 56.7 s); the candidate drift (+12.058 s) is larger
than the B3−A4 effect, so B2 carries most of the gain. All four legs pass every count with 0 faults. This is a
descriptive reading of two whole runs per source, not a significance claim; no extra run, sample removal or per-leg
tuning was used.

### 1.16 Measured on the formation tail 4 head (local, this Mac, memory API; hosted through GitHub Free `hetzner-control` with the API on Postgres)

Two read-only surveys of `0ef6528ed` (main after #689) read the timing-on hosted 50 of §1.15 (run 39505125464)
against #687's run 39410485156. One followed the join chain. The other followed the SDK connect path, the claim
order, the outbound delivery and the session retention. Each owner carries its label: proven from code, measured,
or a strong suspicion.

- **Hosted joins are no-ops** (measured, proven from code; D332). All 50 join attempts on the #689 head, and all 55
  on #687, commit a transaction of exactly 2 statements: the AppInbox completion alone. The persistent hosted store
  already holds the 50 agents as members, so the join computes a no-op. The hosted chain measures a read and a
  result path; the write path is judged by the medium-scale gate and the Postgres tests.
- **A join's cost is contention, not its own work** (measured; D333). A join with no other handler running costs
  154 ms p50 (n = 18; #687 129 ms). Beside about three others it costs 474 ms (n = 22). A least-squares fit gives
  202 ms + 117 ms per other running handler. The mean concurrency beside a join rose 0.70 → 1.61 under the flight.
- **The chain sits idle while a claimed join waits for a slot** (measured; cause proven from code; D334). Over the
  42.683 s chain window the chain is busy 20.995 s. For 10.884 s a claimed join waited for a slot while four
  handlers ran, on average 3.95 of them WS connects. A fresh keyed run waited in turn; only a run released by a
  same-key settlement was preferred (D328).
- **NEW claims follow the sequence's blocks, not arrival** (proven from code; measured; D335). `trans_inbox_seq` is
  `CACHE 100`, and the NEW claim and its context head ordered by `next_ts nulls first, ri_row_id`, so a row's place
  followed its connection's block of 100 ids. On both runs the five slowest SDK connects waited 14.2–16.6 s for a
  ticket handler of 0.02–0.71 s. On the #689 head 11 of 50 tickets were claimed after a younger row (#687: 32 of 50).
- **The WS connect is bound by its round trips** (proven from code; measured; D336). One connect made about 14
  sequential read round trips and 9 in its transaction. Reads are 74 % of the handler's 1.979 s p50 (#687
  1.041 s), and the pool `BEGIN` wait is 4.8 %. Every phase grew about 1.9× together at K = 4. The co-member
  statement costs 250 ms per round trip against 0.327 ms of Postgres execution locally.
- **WS outbound delivery is one serial worker** (measured; the link to the connected-barrier wait is a strong
  suspicion; D337). The run has one `workerId`, 152 batches and 0 overlaps. A 16-member admission group took 2.210 s
  to decide and 7.442 s to commit, and the bundle read its members' commit observations one after another. A
  connect's own pages first settle 26.667 s p50 after its commit (#687 17.009 s). This is off the SDK `connected`
  path.
- **Disconnected sessions stay 30 days and the principal reads scan them** (proven from code; growth on hosted a
  strong suspicion; D338). The disconnect keeps the row for the auth session TTL. The co-member, own-snapshot and
  clients-list reads prefix-scan every session row of a principal and filter in JavaScript. Locally `alice` holds
  37 session rows.
- **After the upgrade the SDK waits on REST** (measured). Upgrade end → `connected` is 3.390 s p50 against
  2.408 s: the ICE GET, the workspace-wide groups and clients list scans (0.610 and 0.435 s p50 on the server,
  against 0.329 and 0.170 s) and browser hydration. No WS delivery gates `connected`.
- **Completion polls load the pool** (measured; their share needs measurement). The hot stretch runs 33–41
  completion polls per second, one status read each.

What landed (D339–D347 and D349), each RED first at its owner:

- **Creation-order NEW claims** (D339, ruling R1). NEW claims and the context-head subquery order by
  `created_ts, ri_row_id`; RETRY keeps due order. One owner, `resolveResourceInboxClaimOrder`, decides it for
  Postgres, in-memory and IndexedDB. A local `EXPLAIN` of the new claim scans `resource_inbox_ix` with no sort. No
  sequence or schema change. With timing on, the `dequeue-pass` line carries the row id and creation time.
- **Chain heads first** (D340, ruling R2). A keyed run waiting in turn is promoted once a same-key successor
  attaches behind it. The option is now `keyedChainsFirst`.
- **Ingress at enqueue, decisions at the attempt** (D341, ruling R3). Authenticated group commands capture their
  ingress facts at enqueue through the winner materializer. The lane neither captures nor persists. Stamps keep the
  enqueue instant; every decision and window uses the lane-sampled `attemptAtEpochMs`.
- **A cheaper join** (D342). The capture reads nothing, the live-session row joins the one-batch read, and the result
  replacement returns the row identity only. On the lane a hosted no-op join goes from 9 statements in 8 round trips
  to 5 in 5 (from code).
- **The strict group queue key** (D343). The removed authority paths leave one ingress shape and one session store.
- **A connect in 7 + 5 statements** (D344). One point batch, one co-member batch and one multi-row outbox insert.
- **A 7-day disconnected-session retention** (D345, ruling R4).
- **Batched commit observations** (D346). A 16-member group reads in 2 store rounds instead of 16.
- **Run-id tagged Postgres test rows** (D347).
- **Absent-peer dials dropped in the browser** (D349; from the local Postgres ALM lane, pre-existing on main). A
  reconcile pass drops the offering side's in-flight dial to a peer that lost presence, so the peer is redialed
  fresh when its presence returns. The answering side keeps its setup.

Owner evidence (real Postgres unless marked):

| Test                                                      | Result                                                                                                                                       |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Claim order, two sessions holding two sequence blocks     | The NEW claim and the context-head claim return the older row; RED returned the younger                                                      |
| Chain head behind twelve slow keyless rows (unit, flight) | Starts at the first settlement after its successor attaches                                                                                  |
| Join read batch                                           | One runtime-state statement, the auth-session row included; missing, mismatched and expired sessions are denied 403 from it (6/6)            |
| Join enqueue ingress                                      | Stored with an `ingress` authority; a forced conflict retry reuses the enqueue-time event id, hash and instant at attempt 2 (1/1)            |
| Result replacement                                        | Returns `ris_row_id` only (1/1)                                                                                                              |
| Authorised WS connect statements                          | 15 → 7 before `BEGIN`, 7 → 5 in the transaction, 3 outbox row ids returned                                                                   |
| Client session retention                                  | 8 days after disconnect purged, 6 days kept until disconnect + 7 d; a stale-generation reconnect stays fenced, a newer one is accepted (2/2) |
| Outbound commit group (unit, turn-batched recorder)       | 16 members, 48 reads in 2 rounds instead of 16                                                                                               |
| Outbound dequeue statements (PGlite)                      | A 10-row group's autocommit statements 47 → 39                                                                                               |
| Join convergence, presence interleaving                   | 6/6 and 2/2                                                                                                                                  |
| Absent-peer dial (unit, dialer fixture)                   | The offering side's in-flight dial is dropped on a presence leave and redialed fresh on return; the answering side keeps its setup (33/33)   |

The frozen native-50 A-B-B-A block, main `0ef6528ed` against the head, timing off, meets the rule for the whole
formation. B2 − A1 is −1.764 s and B3 − A4 −4.935 s: symmetric −3.349 s (−6.5 %) against a +2.603 s baseline

The CPU/GC capture run for the event-loop share (`capture_api_cpu_gc`, timing on, `preserve-public-settings`;

### 1.17 Measured on the formation tail 5 head `71925ed4c` (local, this Mac, memory API; hosted through GitHub Free `hetzner-control` with the API on Postgres)

Three read-only surveys of `34fe4422a` (main after #690) read the timing-on hosted 50 of §1.16 (run 39584426299) and
its CPU/GC capture (run 39586289916). One followed the CPU of a WS connect on the API main thread. One followed the
completion wait's polls and the REST reads after the upgrade. One followed the durable outbound worker. Each owner
carries its label: proven from code, measured, or a strong suspicion.

- **The connect's CPU is mostly rows it drops** (measured; the per-connect row count a strong suspicion; D350). The
  API main thread is 92.7 % busy over the 61.8 s setup window, and Postgres executes 0.3 ms of each 250 ms round
  trip. The 50 connects spend about 9.5 s of it, about 190 ms each, and about 6 s of that decodes rows the connect then
  drops. The co-member read decodes about 1,200 stored session rows per connect (range 800–1,700) and keeps about 25.
  Group snapshot reads decode every presence-session row of the room and keep the ones the summary lists.
- **The decoders' clone protects nothing** (proven from code; measured; D351). `structuredClone` costs 4.974 s self
  and 6.762 s inclusive, 10.9 % of the window. Every stored value is a fresh `JSON.parse` of a `text` column, every
  decoded type is `Readonly`, and the clone runs before a value reaches any cache. On a 644-byte `ClientSession` the
  clone costs 1.85 µs against 0.79 µs for `JSON.parse`. The only store that keeps an object it hands out is the
  test-only `InMemoryClientStateEventStore`.
- **Every read batch is validated twice, with two `TextEncoder` buffers per key pair** (proven from code; measured;
  D352). `validateRuntimeStateReadBatchResult` costs 5.565 s inclusive; its key-order check's `encode` is 1.890 s,
  the whole "text encoding" frame. The repository validates the result and every resolving reader validates it
  again; `requireExactKeys` sorts the keys of every entry (0.660 s). Every runtime-state key is ASCII.
- **The JSON-safety walk runs on `JSON.parse` output** (proven from code; measured; D353). `decodeJsonWireValue`
  costs 3.555 s inclusive. On parsed text it can reject only `-0` and non-finite numbers, which `JSON.stringify` never
  writes; the typed-field validators reject them, the group metadata validator after a check.
- **The strict groups list scans the workspace** (proven from code; measured; D354). `GET …/groups` reads every group,
  member, presence summary and presence row of the workspace in four range reads over three rounds, then keeps the
  caller's groups. It takes 0.807 s p50 (p95 1.350 s) on the SDK `connected` gate and 5.95 s of the thread (9.6 %):
  `readScopeSnapshot` 4.031 s and the route 1.922 s, of which 1.697 s is a fire-and-forget hydration that compares an
  equal revision through two sorted-key stringifications. Upgrade end → SDK end is 3.548 s p50; server REST is about
  0.95 s of it.
- **Every waiter ticks alone** (proven from code; measured; D355). The setup window holds 1,416 completion polls (253
  first reads, 221 wake reads, 942 tick reads), more than all 1,063 transaction statements, 33–53 per second in
  seconds 24–58. Each waiter's 1 s safety tick has its own phase, so no two tick reads share a turn. There are 0
  `missing` observations and 0 spurious wakes; the wake does the useful read. The `wait-retry` lines named every
  pending observation `App inbox entry not found`.
- **The outbound worker is never idle** (measured; the round-trip count proven from code; D356). From 9.37 s to
  56.9 s it runs 27 batches, 50.89 s busy: reads 10.93 s (21.5 %; 3 sequential authority reads and 1 supersedence
  read per row, 2-wide), the group commit 19.58 s (38.5 %), the row-ordered dispatch 12.49 s (24.6 %) and the
  release 3.88 s (7.6 %). Every WS_OUTBOX row has one AL sender, the server peer. A connect's pages first settle
  26.354 s p50 after its commit, 24.857 s of it waiting for the claim. The last two topology frames were claimed 8.28
  and 11.97 s after their commits (their link to readiness a strong suspicion).
- **The connect's commit announces nothing** (proven from code; measured; D357). The WS connect writes WS_OUTBOX rows
  without waking the outbound queue, so an idle worker claims them 0.54 / 2.67 s (p50 / max) later; the first connect
  of the run waited 2.17 s.

What landed (D362–D374 and D379–D383), each RED first at its owner, under the maintainer's rulings R1–R4 (D358–D361):

- **Decode once** (D362, D363; ruling R4). The nine persisted-state decoders return the validated value; the in-memory
  event store copies at append. Stored-row reads parse without the JSON-safety walk, including the stored idempotency
  and authority-guard reads; the write side and every wire decoder keep it. The validators reject `-0` and non-finite
  numbers in typed fields and in group metadata through one `requireUnitRate` and the existing primitives, typed on
  one `ValidationValue` / `ValidationRecord`.
- **The read batch validated once, without allocation** (D364). The Postgres repository maps driver rows straight to
  typed entries and asserts the result once (`assertRuntimeStateReadBatchResult`); the resolvers, and the topology
  mutation read, trust it. One `compareRuntimeStateKeys` gives C collation in code-point order without a buffer.
- **Only live rows read** (D365, D366; ruling R3). The read batch gains `prefix-containing` (stored-value containment
  on the key-range scan), used by the co-member read with `{"status":"active","disconnectedAtEpochMs":null}`, and
  `prefix-expiring-after` (an `expire_at_ts` floor from the purge rule), used by the snapshot, scope and list reads of
  presence sessions. The own read and the mutation reads keep their full row sets. No index, no migration.
- **The strict groups list from the principal's groups** (D367, D368). The route serves the caller's groups through
  the principal read the WS connect uses, sorted into group-key order; the non-strict path keeps the scan. Hydration
  skips a revision the state-sync repository already holds, and `jsonEquals` decides plain JSON trees structurally.
- **Coalesced completion polls** (D369–D372). One instance-wide safety tick settles every parked wait in one turn,
  the Postgres entries repository batches the status reads of one turn into one statement, a reservation winner skips
  its immediate read, and a pending observation is named `App inbox entry is not complete: <observation>`.
- **The outbound group's reads in two rounds, and the connect's wake** (D373, D374; rulings R1, R2). A dequeued
  group reads its authority and supersedence in two rounds instead of 3 + 1 sequential reads per row; the connect's
  commit wakes the outbound queue. The flight and the dispatch order are unchanged.
- **The presence summary worker's stall** (D379–D383; from the CI medium-scale red below). A draining pass gives
  each lane at most 8 claims (`DRAINING_LANE_CLAIMS_PER_PASS`); a coalesced outbox write that finds its row present
  returns a typed outcome; the summary claim coalesces due RETRY siblings; a coalesced page is bounded by the AL
  payload limit; the replan verdict is read and computed before the transaction, whose guard checks the observed
  successor.

Owner evidence (real Postgres unless marked):

| Test                                                 | Result                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Persisted-state decoders (unit)                      | Return the validated reference; RED was `Object.is` inequality for the group and both client decoders; the corruption suites stay green (19/19)                           |
| Typed fields once the walk is gone (unit)            | `-0` in five typed fields and `Infinity`, `-0` and a nested `-Infinity` in metadata are rejected; RED 8 of 9 accepted; stored-text `-0` / `1e400` rejected on both sides  |
| Stored session decode (deno micro-bench, not a test) | 4.5 → 1.47 µs per decode (−67 %)                                                                                                                                          |
| Read batch validation count (unit)                   | One read validates once, the topology mutation read too; RED `called 2 times`                                                                                             |
| Co-member sessions                                   | A principal with 40 disconnected sessions and 1 connected returns 1 row from the same single statement; RED `[ 42 ]`; `EXPLAIN` keeps the key-range index condition       |
| Presence floor                                       | A presence row past its lease + 24 h is left out of the snapshot read and kept by the mutation read; RED `expected 3 to be 1`; the own read keeps its disconnected target |
| Strict groups list (Postgres, Deno, black-box)       | Same set and order as scan + filter for member, non-member, banned, invited, left, removed, a deleted group, two workspaces and a prefix id; RED the strict list scanned  |
| Equal-revision hydration (unit, Deno)                | A held revision is not hydrated; an equal revision with different content still is; no `JSON.stringify` or `localeCompare` in the decision; RED 2 serializations          |
| Completion status batch                              | Keys read in one turn share one statement on `resource_inbox_unique_k` (no seq scan); a corrupt row fails only its own waiter; RED 6 statements for one turn              |
| Shared safety tick (unit)                            | N waiters resume in one turn, no tick while nothing waits, a budget tail keeps its own timer; RED 4 tests                                                                 |
| Reservation winner across instances (PGlite)         | Returns within 300 ms of the remote handler, under one tick; RED 982 ms                                                                                                   |
| Outbound authority rounds (unit, round gate)         | A 16-row raw group reads in 2 rounds instead of 24; on the generic lane 8 authority turns → 1                                                                             |
| Outbound dequeue statements (PGlite)                 | A 10-row group's autocommit statements 39 → 35; the WS read phase 39 → 3 statements; a corrupt row fails alone and charges the breaker once                               |
| Connect wake (unit)                                  | `commit`, `mutation.write`, `wake`, `observe`; an expiry page that writes its successor still wakes                                                                       |
| Draining-pass lane share (unit)                      | A stream of 40 re-appearing RETRY rows: the first pass claims 8 and the NEW row is handled 9th; RED `expected 40 to be less than 40`                                      |
| Coalesced outbox conflicts                           | A present successor and a lost head race are typed outcomes; a foreign row under the successor key and a duplicate plain insert are still corruption                      |
| Payload-bound summary page (unit, PGlite)            | 64 connects with the widest ids commit in two pages, every delta within `payloadBytes`, the summary lists all 64; RED `AL payload exceeds the byte limit`                 |
| Transaction-write check                              | 38 blocking findings on `9a47a7415` → 0; 84 advisory, as the parent                                                                                                       |

The medium-scale stall (measured from the CI artifacts; the mechanism proven from code; D375–D378). The CI medium-scale
gate failed on `4f336adb0` (run 39631767313): through the secondary, groupFive's presence summary showed 61 of 101
sessions in all five convergence polls, because no server claimed its `app-outbox.group-presence-summary` rows for 38.8
s. Nothing in the failing path runs code that commit changed. The base's own green runs near-missed the same way 2 in 3
times (28–35 s stalls, recovered at poll attempt 4 and 5). The draining APP_OUTBOX pass had no per-lane budget, so a
lane claimed until a claim found nothing; 348 successor-key collisions failed as corruption and re-dued at 1–16 ms, and
a failed page's siblings were released to RETRY at 1 ms and never coalesced again, so the RETRY lane fed itself and the
NEW lane never ran on any process. The stall ended when one process's outbox breaker opened silently; its next pass
claimed groupFive's 97 rows in one page, which threw `AL payload exceeds the byte limit`. The CI gate passes on
`da82e8471` and `2f8830977`. Locally on `9a47a7415` (three API processes on Postgres, outbox pass timing on) it passes,
2,842 passed and 0 failed in 129.2 s: the longest summary claim gap is 4.44 s (red 38.83 s), the other groups' 3.58–3.68
s (red 7.60–8.80 s); summary created → claim is 114 / 1,167 / 4,336 ms p50 / p95 / max over 313 rows; no lane claims
more than 8 in any of 222 passes (NEW reaches 8 in 104, RETRY peaks at 5) and the next pass follows a budget-hit pass
after 2.6 ms p50 / 25.7 ms max; corruption errors 348 → 0, payload-limit errors 1 → 0, all 313 summary writes succeed;
every group converges on poll attempt 1, its 101-recipient write 2.2–2.5 s before the first poll; the largest coalesced
claim is 80 rows. The run is on a faster machine than the runner (109 s against 190 s of runner time), so it shows the
stall gone, not a like-for-like speed. The same timing-on run on main `34fe4422a` on this machine (fresh database, 2,842
passed and 0 failed in 128.0 s) does not reproduce the stall either (longest summary claim gap 2.9 s, every group on
poll attempt 1), so the local pair shows how the pass changed, not a red turning green; the CI gate is the proof. Main →
head: summary created → claim 1,410 / 3,947 / 8,267 → 114 / 1,167 / 4,336 ms p50 / p95 / max; summary RETRY rows 199 →
10; corruption errors 11 → 0; the most claims in one pass, NEW / RETRY, 369 / 128 → 8 / 5; the longest claiming pass
17.6 → 1.77 s. The three busy non-summary outbox topics wait 0.7–0.9 s longer at the median and less at the tail (p95
7.7 → 4.5 s). The outbox is saturated at about 19 rows/s per server in both runs and the mean first-attempt queue age is
unchanged (2,444 → 2,446 ms), so the bounded share redistributes the wait rather than adding to it; the fixed cost per
pass is about 0 (statements per row 29.2 → 31.9) (measured; D387). The CI gate is green on `da82e8471`, `2f8830977`,
`d18b642ad`, `d3f08929d` and `10b167e1a`, whose production code is that of `d3f08929d` (it removes two stale
reviewed-disposition rows). Every lane, this medium-scale run, the hosted 15/30/50 and the A-B-B-A B legs ran that code.
The final head `71925ed4c` adds one change to it, the batched status read's ordinal result (D388), and the timing-on run
and the CPU/GC capture deployed it.

The 50 run deleted 16 peers and logged 10 lane errors (0 on #690's 50 runs). The churn is benign (measured; the link to

present before the churn. Whether the faster server makes a three-layout join likelier at 50, and whether that explains
the 0.823 s connected → accepted, needs the timing-on run or repeated 50s.
