The CPU/GC capture (`capture_api_cpu_gc`, timing on), against the targets of a busy share under 80 % (from 92.7 %) and
no `structuredClone` under the decoders: run 39676419651 (`hetzner-control-cpu-gc`, `preserve-public-settings`,
production `71925ed4c`; the capture is complete and the profile valid, with a ±267 ms clock mapping). Over the 37.278 s
setup window (#690 61.755 s) the API main thread is busy 80.4 % (79.8–80.5 % within the mapping uncertainty; #690 92.7
%; idle 7.3 → 19.6 %), so the under-80 % target is **not met**, by 0.4 points. `structuredClone` is 0.1 % and gone from
the persisted group, member, presence-session and client-session decoders (the 0.029 s left is
`decodeAuthMutationIntent` and `decodeAuthMutationResult`): **met**. The JSON-safety walk falls 5.6 → 0.9 %,
`validateEntry` and `toOrderedJson` to 0, text encoding 4.8 → about 1.6 %, `readScopeSnapshot` 4.03 → 0 s and
`readGroupSnapshotList` 1.92 → 0.83 s. The top self frames are now `writeBuffer` (the Postgres wire) 6.4 %, with the
driver about 14.5 % of the window, `toLiveGroupStateEntryValue` 3.1 %, `exact` 1.6 % and storage-key decoding 3.5 % (an
unchanged share): the Postgres wire and the remaining group-state parsing are the next owners of the busy share. GC is
2.9 % by the trace's pause sum (99 pauses, 1.085 s, max 35 ms) and 5.9 % by the profiler (#690 4.5 / 9.8 %). A
per-connect CPU figure is not readable from the frames, because the stacks break at each `await`.

The state-write comparison on the head: two benches, a fresh migrated database per leg, order main, head, head, main.
Bench 1 (main `34fe4422a` against `10b167e1a`): throughput rises on every workload but one hot leg, uncontended 246.7 /
239.7 → 254.4 / 258.7 mutations/s (+3.1 / +8.0 %), shared 216.6 / 205.3 → 229.7 / 229.7 (+6.0 / +11.9 %), hot 45.5 /
50.3 → 53.4 / 50.0 (+17.3 / −0.6 %), with statements and rows read flat; the control main against main flags shared −5.2
%, the noise floor. Main-1 against head-1 flagged the uncontended median `serializedResultBytes` +5.1 % (15.66 → 16.45
MB) at flat statements and rows: the batched status read echoed each row's key, about 204 B a row, on the poll the bench
issues every 1–5 ms without a subscription (D388). Bench 2 on `71925ed4c`, with ordinal mapping: 15.66 → 15.73 MB (+0.4
%), the flag gone. That pair is noisy (the control flags itself on six metrics, hot throughput −8.5 %), and one contrast
flags hot statements +7.3 %, load and retry dependent where bench 1 was flat. Bench 1 carries the throughput verdict and
bench 2 the bytes fix.

### 1.18 Measured on the formation tail 6 head `aeed54640` (local, this Mac, memory API unless marked; hosted through GitHub Free `hetzner-control` with the API on Postgres)

Three read-only surveys of `133df2c0d` (main after #691) read the timing-on hosted 50 of §1.17 (run 39674474258) and
its CPU/GC capture (run 39676419651). One followed the durable outbound worker. One followed the AppInbox lane and the
completion wait. One followed the API thread, the strict groups list and the browser tail. Each owner carries its
label: proven from code, measured, or a strong suspicion.

- **The outbound worker is the saturated stage** (measured; the round trips counted by probe; D389). It is never idle
  in the 22.05 s hot stretch. A connect's pages settle 13.796 s p50 after its commit, 11.751 s of it waiting for the
  claim. Decision plus bundle is 12.86 s of the hot stretch (58.3 %), the floor of the one-sender commit chain. A
  16-row batch costs about 164 sequential round trips: the bundle transaction 83 (33 state inserts, 16 hold locks, 32
  queue-row inserts, `BEGIN` and `COMMIT`), the dispatch 48 (a `pg_notify` and two reads per row, the captured-policy
  re-read), the decision 8 and the release 18.
- **Most completion polls are the safety net's** (measured; D390). The 779 `wait-poll` lines are 489 tick reads in
  about 17 rounds, 240 own-commit wakes that each read the status and then the result row, and 50 first reads: a WS
  connect's enqueue woke the queue before its waiter subscribed. All 240 wakes read a terminal row.
- **The claimed-join idle is one priority inversion** (measured; the mechanism proven from code; D390). 1.540 s of
  the 1.579 s is one episode: a `GROUP_ACTIVATION_STATUS` run keyed `<group>` waited in turn while the joins keyed
  `<group>:aggregate` waited behind it by prefix overlap. A waiting run was promoted only by an equal-key successor.
- **Fragment statements leak, and list predicates scan** (proven from code; measured; D391).
  `PSqlFragment.toStatement()` built a new strings array per call, and postgres.js keeps one origin `Error` per
  strings array for the life of the process: `cachedError` 0.306 s, and the old generation grows 59 → 131 MB in
  200 s. `in ${sql(list)}` runs the driver's keyword regex over the statement prefix (0.484 s). The strict groups list
  makes about 7 serial statements.
- **Keys decode twice, and audiences validate four times** (proven from code; measured; D391). The child storage-key
  decode is 1.541 s inclusive: two splits, four decodes and seven encodes per row. The WS connect re-validates the
  audience snapshots the assembler already validated (0.875 s).
- **The sign-in probe scans the scope** (measured; D391). `GET …/stats/me/realtime` reads up to 100 groups with 3
  statements each, per agent: `listSnapshotsPage` 0.972 s inclusive.
- **An RTT-only replan made the third layout** (measured; D391). The first `RTC_RTT_SUBMIT` came at 28.90 s, after
  readiness at 22.06 s, and promoted layout v390 at 29.39 s with presence unchanged. The third layout restarted the
  recipe's stable-layout loop (+2.7 to +5.4 s) and set off the 15 s transition-grace churn.

What landed (D395–D402), each RED first at its owner, under the maintainer's rulings R1–R3 (D392–D394), with the
review rulings (D403, D404), the plan-cache fix found after the hosted runs (D405–D408) and one harness fix (D409):

- **The bundle in three statements** (D395). A commit names the sender version as its fence (`setFence`). The
  Postgres buffer makes it the guard of one guarded multi-row state write, then takes the holds in one ordered
  `for update` and inserts the queue rows in one multi-row insert. Every writer of the version row names it as its
  fence, so each locks it first; a commit names one fence, and a fence that writes no row throws.
- **The dispatch reads nothing after the commit** (D396). Each committed row hands the policy its commit wrote to
  the publication. The global row order and the publish before the send stay, and the recipient authority is still
  validated on the handed policy.
- **The decision in one round, the release in one statement** (D397). A group's decision reads go out in one turn
  per read depth on at most two connections. A batch's release is one `update … from jsonb_to_recordset` with the
  per-row compare; a lost row rolls the whole update back.
- **Chain heads, result reads on a wake, subscribe before the wake** (D398; R2 as revised). A waiting run is promoted,
  and marked a chain head, only by an equal or extending successor. A completion wake reads the result row directly.
  The enqueue writes without waking, subscribes, and only a winner wakes the queue; the queue reports whether it
  created the row (`writeIfAbsent`).
- **The SQL wire** (D399). Fragment strings are interned per text; list predicates take one array parameter
  (`= any($n::text[])`); the strict list's cached path selects by member rows and fences the second round on the
  group row's `snapshotVersion`; a timing line and a per-shape statement census run with timing on only.
- **Keys decoded once, audiences validated once** (D400). One pass over the parts checks each part's canonical form
  once. The connect trusts the assembler's validation and keeps the scope check.
- **The RTT re-plan hold** (D401; R1). An RTT-only recompute that would replace an active layout younger than
  `topology.planning.rttReplanMinimumLayoutAgeMs` (15 000 ms) commits `held-young-layout` and keeps the stored layout.
- **The sign-in probe** (D402; R3). The step reads the workspace's `clients` collection, one point read of the
  session's own client under strict reads.
- **Recordset statements planned per execution, one-element sets as equality** (D405–D408). The release, the hold
  lock and the key read go out unprepared, so PostgreSQL plans each execution with its parameters; a one-element type
  or status set claims through `= $n` again.

Owner evidence (real Postgres unless marked):

| Test                                          | Result                                                                                                                                                                              |
| --------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Outbound bundle round trips                   | A 16-row WS group's bundle runs 3 statements inside `BEGIN … COMMIT` (a 33-row guarded state batch, a 16-row hold lock, a 32-row insert); RED 81; the PGlite pin 81 → 3             |
| Version-fence lock order                      | A bundle fenced on the version and a control commit of the same sender: the bundle commits, the control commit answers a typed conflict; RED `40P01 deadlock detected`              |
| Fences per commit (memory, IndexedDB, PGlite) | A second fence in one commit throws and writes nothing; RED memory and IndexedDB resolved                                                                                           |
| Outbound dispatch statements                  | 16 statements after the 16th commit, all autocommit `select pg_notify(?, ?)`, in row order, 16 frames delivered; RED 48                                                             |
| Decision reads (unit)                         | A 16-member group reads in 1 round per read depth, at most two connections; RED 8 rounds                                                                                            |
| Batch release                                 | A mixed batch releases in 1 update with each row equal to `computeResourceInboxRelease`; a lost middle row rolls it back; RED `expected 16 to be 1`                                 |
| Exclusion-keyed promotion (unit)              | A waiting `<group>` run is preferred when `<group>:aggregate` attaches; a wider `scope` or `scope:g` promotes nothing; RED of the inverse `expected 'presence-0' to be 'ws-ticket'` |
| Winner from the repository (unit)             | A replay under a deterministic message factory reports `winner: false`; RED `winner: true`                                                                                          |
| Interned fragments (unit)                     | 1,000 `toStatement()` calls give 1 strings array; RED `expected 1000 to be 1`                                                                                                       |
| List predicates                               | `= any` returns the in-list's rows; `EXPLAIN` on 12,000 rows keeps every index; RED 17 / 4 / 6 driver helper calls                                                                  |
| Strict group list statements                  | Kept groups in 3 statements (RED 5), a renamed, joined or removed group in 4; a group renamed between the rounds is read again; a purged group 3 (RED 4)                            |
| Storage-key decoding (unit)                   | 266 accepted keys decode to their parts; 572 single-fault keys rejected with the old messages; 8 multi-fault keys keep the old order                                                |
| Connect read assertions (unit)                | 2 per connect, the compute and the validating recompute; RED `expected 3 to be 2`                                                                                                   |
| RTT re-plan hold                              | A slow-edge refresh at +500 ms leaves the snapshot and fingerprint untouched, at +15 s it promotes; unit RED `expected 'accepted' to be 'held-young-layout'`                        |
| Recordset plans                               | Grown 200 → 20,000 rows without `analyze`, the release, the lock and the key read make 0 sequential scans; RED 1 each                                                               |
| Claim plan                                    | A one-type, one-status claim on 25,000 rows is an Index Scan on `resource_inbox_ix`, with no Seq Scan or Bitmap Heap Scan; RED a BitmapOr and Bitmap Heap Scan                      |
| Production adapter (Deno)                     | A marked statement reaches `unsafe(…, { prepare: false })` at the root and in a transaction, counted under its cached shape; RED `undefined`                                        |
| Transaction-write check                       | 0 blocking, 97 advisory (99 on main)                                                                                                                                                |

## 2. GitHub Free review

The repository is public, so standard-runner minutes are free; the hard limits are ≈20 concurrent
jobs per account (the workflow caps agent jobs at 19 and needs every shard at once), 4 vCPU/16 GiB
standard runners (larger runners are a paid plan), one shared concurrency group across both modes
and the Hetzner workflow, and the `production` environment gate.

| Manifest | `hetzner-control` (GitHub browsers → Hetzner API)                                                                 | `all-local` (one runner)                                                               |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 19 (15)  | Practical now; ran end-to-end and failed at setup for the server-side reasons in §1.5.                            | Practical to dispatch; 6 agents pass, 11 and 15 fail on 4 vCPU.                        |
| 20 (30)  | Input changes only (`agents_per_job=2`, `max_parallel_jobs=15`, idle account); same server blocker, larger burst. | Dispatchable, not practical on Free.                                                   |
| 21 (50)  | Input changes only (the workflow defaults, 18 concurrent jobs); same server blocker.                              | Not possible on Free: needs a larger or self-hosted runner and a production-built SPA. |

Verdict: GitHub is not what blocks the cohorts; the Hetzner API's setup burst and ACK return path
are. The recipes run locally first through the all-local lifecycle (`CI=1`, memory API, headless
SPA, mesh min size N+1, unbounded retention; the runbook's single command omits the `--admit`
step and four required variables and is corrected in step 2a). On this Mac (12 cores, 96 GiB) 15
agents fit easily, 30 is plausible, 50 fits in memory; the API main thread stays the bottleneck.
Measured on the step 2a head (local, this Mac): the 15-agent run passes (15/15 recipes, 231/231
assertions); the 30- and 50-agent runs fail at setup because every co-located agent registers and
logs in from one IP and the API's per-IP limits are 20 registrations and 30 logins per minute
(`authentication.rateLimits.registrationIp`/`loginIp`): the last 10 and 30 registrations are
refused, the page shows the refusal, the worker still registers the agent with the control server,
and the recipe's first login reports a misleading 401. The lifecycle's API env block raised only
the per-user login limit; so does the hosted controller's env, which co-locates agents on one VM.
Step 2a raises the two per-IP limits in both co-located lifecycles to the cohort ceiling (product
defaults unchanged) and makes a refused registration fail the agent at start.

Changes that only the maintainer can make and that the review recommends regardless: a deployment
branch policy or required reviewer on the `production` environment (any branch can read its secrets
today), deleting the plaintext Actions variable named like the password secret, and optionally a
`runs-on` input for a self-hosted all-local runner.

## 3. Decisions

| Id   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| D194 | Step 2b: the AppInbox dequeue treats a handler-finalized success as a typed value: the success releaser skips the post-commit release transaction for entries whose completion was written inside the handler transaction (the failure path already does this through `ResourceInboxHandlerEntryError`); the non-transactional result path keeps the normal release; the completion notification and telemetry still fire.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| D195 | Step 2b: the engine is woken by the registration runnable after a batch that claimed at least one entry (`engine.wake()`, the engine's documented contract) for both the inbox and the outbox registrations, so a long handler is followed by the next claim at once instead of after the idle backoff.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| D196 | Step 2b: breaker and pass semantics for the AppInbox follow the ALM lanes: typed retryable conflicts are not breaker failures; while the breaker is open the task reports no work and schedules `wakeAt` the reopen time; one task's `isWork` error is reported through `reportTaskFailure` and does not abort the pass; a reserve or release error returns from the lane with `wakeAt(now + backoff)` instead of sleeping inside the slot.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| D197 | Step 2b: the AppInbox TIMEOUT lane recovers a stale reservation after a 60 s lease (a value in the AppInbox options, above the hosted p99 handler time); finalization recovery stays at 5 min; `resource_inbox_results` joins the existing expiry eviction loop. A stale finisher still fails the attempt CAS, so a shorter lease changes recovery time, not correctness.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| D198 | Step 2b: in single-process profiles the same-process completion notification is the primary completion path: `prod-in-memory` raises `appInbox.completionWait.maxRetryIntervalMs` so polling is the fallback. As applied: the conflict's delay caps the queue's backoff at 100 ms, so the first retries stay as fast as before and only the 1–8 s steps from the sixth attempt shrink; locally no summary row lost its write more than four times, so the cap shows only on a slow runner.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

## 4. Slices

### Step 2a: the leader-ACK path and the harness amplification

Production, shared: D202 at its owners (`packages/shared/alm/inbound/admission/compute-al-inbound-admission.ts`,
`packages/shared/alm/inbound/al-inbound-effect-intent.ts` and `transition-al-pending-ack.ts`,
`packages/shared/alm/inbound/read-al-inbound-work-selection.ts`,
`packages/shared/queuebox/in-memory-queue-box.ts`,
`packages/shared/alm/outbound/control/al-outbound-receipt-admission.ts`,
`packages/shared/services/ws-queue-box-server/ws-queue-box-server-ack-relay.ts`).
Harness: D199 at `apps/rallar-black-box-control-server/src` and the shared-test evidence index;
D200 at `packages/shared-test/rallar-bb-test/{runtime,control-client,wait}` and
`apps/rallar-black-box-headless`. Docs: the runbook's complete local command and the GitHub Free
verdict.

Each task is RED first: a leader receipt completes `acknowledged` with the director as an internal
node and a silent leaf below it; a leader-mode relay produces no upward ACK for a non-audience
child, and the origin receives one control per shot; `send-control` claims before 15 releasable
rows on the volatile lane; the in-memory QueueBox finds finalizations without a full scan; a receipt surviving three commit
conflicts reaches `acknowledged`; 61 valid leader ACK relays in a minute are all published and a
failed NOTIFY returns `Left`; one rollup per control message with a counted evidence index; a
page's per-event work independent of history size; an oversize frame recorded once with a gap
record. Measured: the local all-local 15-agent run before (240 delivery-state timeouts, failed)
and after, in the same regime.

### Step 2b: the engine, its fault tolerance, the handler cost, the director-rooted tree and the diagnostic volume, then 15 → 30 → 50

D194 to D198 at the queuebox engine and the AppInbox registration (statement count after the
handler commit 4 → 0; the fake-clock engine's second claim at 1,000 ms, not ≥1,600 ms; eleven typed
conflicts leave dequeue allowed and an open breaker produces no passes; a throwing `isWork` on one
task still submits another task's work; a throwing reservator costs no in-slot sleep; a stale
AppInbox reservation reclaimed after the lease; the results repository evicted), then D201 (the
sync digest's known vectors and a `setTimeout(0)` sentinel that never fires during
compute + validate; `computeStateSnapshotPages` once per compute; `validateComputedProjection` once
per validation while every tamper case still rejects); then D205 (a director among 16 members roots the tree; an
appointment after an accepted layout produces a director-rooted rebuild), D206 (refuted by a probe: forwarding already runs in the committing batch; withdrawn),
D207 (the canonical scale recipe declares `almDiagnostics: false`, the diagnostic one `true`, every
assertion keeps its evidence) and D208 (a refused hand-off produces one diagnostic; the stats block decodes
the three new counters). Then the original capture-off fifteen
acceptance before thirty and thirty before fifty, locally and then through the hosted manifests,
and the four-arm lane-policy benefit gate the plan already binds. Mutation-path changes carry the
state-write comparator and the medium-scale gate.

### Step 2c: the acknowledgement tail at scale, the control plane at 50, then 15 → 30 → 50 hosted

D209–D213, each RED first: twenty-one ACK hand-offs inside one second all leave (today the
twenty-first waits for a bucket); a leader shot at depth 3 produces one attempt and no copy on the
sibling subtrees; a composite result event carries counts and no child results while the root
envelope still nests; a redispatched command joins its running execution and arrives at each
barrier once; the setup-complete barrier follows a readiness whose layout no newer plan replaced.
Measured on the local 50-agent run (window unchanged): shot-ACK p50 by depth, attempts per shot,
`rateLimited`, barrier resolution latency and the end-ACK margin, then 15/30 locally, then hosted
15, 30 and 50 through `Run GitHub Free Distributed Recipe` in `hetzner-control` mode.

### Step 2d: hosted formation throughput, then 30 → 50 hosted

The measured bound on the hosted plane (§1.9) is the single-lane `prod-in-memory` API: an
offer→answer relay takes 4.3 s at 15 and 7.1 s at 30 agents (0.17/0.45/0.93 s locally at
15/30/50), RTC formation 22.5 s and 52–59 s per agent, and the director-rooted rebuild after the
appointment re-forms the overlay behind the same queue while 5 s status reads time out. Static
analysis first: where a WS signal relay, a presence update and a status read wait (the serial
AppInbox lane, the WS outbox worker's wake cadence, the audience reads), then one change at a
time at the owner (a per-key or per-lane concurrency for the in-memory profile, F4, or a relay
path that does not queue behind mutations), judged by the hosted 30-agent run; then 50 and the
four-arm lane-policy benefit gate. The 15/30/50 recipes themselves need no further harness change
for this: they pass locally at 50 and hosted at 15.

**Survey result (scratch `survey-2d-server.md`, `survey-2d-formation.md`).** A signal is not
relayed live at ingress: the server builds its WS inbound runtime without the volatile store pair,
so every best-effort signal falls into the durable PGlite lane (≈9 admission reads, a write
transaction, a claim, a delivery read, a release: ≈20 statements) on PGlite's single exclusive
mutex, FIFO behind every AppInbox transaction and every snapshot list. The relay itself is a direct
socket send. Per-statement CPU on the hosted VM is 4–30× this Mac's and the queue depth grows
with the cohort, which is the whole hosted ratio; the profile is the same locally. Formation-side,
dialing is parallel and unpaced and native ICE/DTLS takes under 0.6 s per edge; over 95 % of an
edge's time is two relays. Two join-driven full rebuilds (a delta larger than
`max(2, ceil(prev/4))`) and the appointment's leader-rooting rebuild churned most edges, and the
harness's status refresh lists the whole workspace. F4 (per-key AppInbox concurrency) would add no
CPU on a single-threaded PGlite and is not taken. Decisions D219–D224; each RED first: a relayed
signal costs zero database statements; an additions-only delta keeps every existing edge; a plan
with an unchanged leader keeps its adjacency; the director's appointment precedes the players'
connect in the scale setup; the status refresh issues one group read. Judged by the hosted
30-agent run (offers ≤ 35 for 29 edges, offer→answer p50 under 1 s, zero status timeouts), then
50, then the four-arm lane-policy benefit gate.

### Step 2e: the hosted lane's per-player cost, then 30 → 50 hosted again

Measured on the local 50-agent run with the AppInbox phase timing on (proposal scratch table): the
handlers are cheap (join 36 ms, presence connect 126 ms, WS connect 15 ms, session heartbeat 28 ms,
RTT submission 30 ms) but the single lane is saturated for the whole setup, so a join waits 6.5 s
p50 and a presence connect 6.0 s behind everything enqueued before it; the completion-wait
subscription itself works (an idle-lane join completes in 17 ms end to end). The survey found six
serial items per player, an authorization capture repeated inside every group mutation, an O(N²)
presence follow-on, no-op client heartbeats that still pay an O(N) read, three PBKDF2 derivations
per agent, and 341 RTT submissions that each list every measurement. Decisions D233–D238, each RED
first and judged by the local timing table (lane items per player 6 → 4, presence-connect handler
p50 126 → under 50 ms, heartbeats that write, RTT items ≤ one per session per round) and then the
hosted 50-agent `rtc.connect` p50 (64.9 s) and sign-in p50 (12.2 s); D239–D241 record what is not
taken.

The first local rerun after D233–D238 (branch `bd5bf4d81`, 50 agents): presence-connect handler p50
126 → 31 ms, join 36 → 26 ms, lane items in the setup window 305 → 254, handler plus transaction time
12.4 → 6.1 s; yet the window stayed 17.5 s because the gap between lane items grew (p50 13.6 → 25.5 ms,
7.6 → 12.3 s in total) and route authorization slowed (64 → 84 ms per call): the fixed statements of a
dequeue pass, queued on the one executor, now dominate. The presence coalescing (D235) claimed one row
every time locally, since the lane releases connects one at a time. Hence D242 and D243.

### Step 2f: the hosted lane's gaps and the overlay acceptance, then 30 → 50 hosted

Measured on the step 2e hosted 50 (proposal scratch survey; the run's API and control journals): the
players' connect p50 53.4 s is 27.7 s behind the lane plus 24.0 s waiting for the one accepted
layout; inside the 59 s setup window the lane's own work is 25 s and 41 s are gaps where the single
thread serves route-side statements (authorization 20 s, ≈350 REST reads, 1,262 waiter re-polls);
50 heartbeats land inside the window; the presence coalescing never fires; six superseded promotions
and two heartbeat 500s are typed outcomes thrown as errors. Decisions D250–D259, each RED first and
judged by the hosted 50 `readiness.waitedMs` p50 (24.0 s → ≤ 5 s), the players' connect p50
(53.4 s → ≤ 35 s), the join queue age (8.9 s → ≤ 6 s), the inter-item gap p50 (74 ms → ≤ 40 ms),
zero heartbeat items and zero superseded rejections in the setup window, and the four-arm gate's
verdict; D260 records what is deferred.

### Step 2g: the API thread's per-player cost and the lane's fault tolerance, then 15 → 30 → 50 hosted

Measured on the step 2f hosted 50 (run 38844516264) and hosted 30 (run 38845227516): the setup chain is the
whole 62 s to traffic-ready (setup-complete to traffic-ready is 0.26 s). The players' connect p50 37.0 s is 27.1 s
before readiness plus a 10.0 s readiness wait. The lane is no longer the bound: it is 11 % busy in the connect
window with a 7 ms gap p50 and queue ages of 0.5–1.4 s (6–13 s on step 2e). The per-player cost has moved into
request time the server never records (D266), and it scales at 0.65 s per player. Sign-in derives 100 passwords at
128 ms each. Formation ends 5.5 s after the last presence connect because the replan debounce can be extended
indefinitely. A fixed 3.5 s quiet wait sits in every recipe. Faults: auth intents enqueued twice, a wedged handler
can hold the lane, a double completion publish, connect events pushed before their socket is registered, and
conflicts thrown with stacks. Decisions D266–D277, each RED first at its owner, judged by the hosted 50 players'
connect p50 (37.0 s → ≤ 25 s), sign-in p50 (10.7 s → ≤ 6 s), the time from the last presence connect to the
accepted publication and all agents ready (5.5 s → ≤ 2 s), traffic-ready (62.3 s → ≤ 45 s) and zero connect events
without a recipient; 15, 30 and 50 run locally first, then hosted in that order on one head. D278 records what moves
to step 2h with the V1c closure.

### Formation tail 2: pipelined admission, the connect's statements and held offers, then 15 → 30 → 50 hosted

Measured on main after #675 (§1.14): the AppInbox NEW lane served K = 4 rows per slowest handler; the engine slept
through wakes a busy slot absorbed (4.561 s of a 6.677 s local setup window idle while rows waited); the
authorized-WS connect wrote 15 statements and probed 21 audience groups one at a time; the co-member read carried
never-expiring instance rows; the prefix-suffix member read scanned the workspace; a held inbound offer waited out
its 1–4 s retry stage after its layout arrived; and the harness polled the API every 0.200 s per agent. Decisions
D306–D314, each RED first at its owner, are judged by the local 50 queue age per command type with phase timing,
the statements and rows per handler, a held offer applied within one engine pass of adoption, local 15 → 30 → 50
and then the uninstrumented hosted 15 → 30 → 50 on one head, one frozen native-50 A-B-B-A block main against the
head with timing off, the state-write comparison and the medium-scale gate. D315–D317 record what is held back
until measured, D318 closes the #675 hot throughput decline, D319 moves the measured cap, K and pool change to the
next slice, and D320 records the block's verdict: the end of formation improves in both contrasts, the whole
formation does not meet the benefit rule.

### Formation tail 3: a persistent AppInbox flight, presence off the group chain and one join read, then 15 → 30 → 50 hosted

Measured on main after #687 (§1.15): an AppInbox pass served each context once, claimed at most 8 and drained before
the next pass; a group row waiting on its key counted against the hold of 4 while it held no slot; slot admission
was first in, first out; the recovery lanes waited for NEW to drain; presence commands waited behind joins only
because they shared the group's key; and a join read in three statements over two steps. Decisions D321–D324, each
RED first at its owner, are judged by the local 50 queue age per command type and the lane's pass and occupancy
figures with phase timing, Postgres proofs of per-context order and key order on the production lane, local
15 → 30 → 50, the local Postgres ALM lane, then the uninstrumented hosted 15 → 30 → 50 on one head, one frozen
native-50 A-B-B-A block main against the head with timing off, one timing-on hosted 50 for occupancy and the group
chain, the state-write comparison and the medium-scale gate. D325 keeps the pool at five against the pool-only
A-B-B-A, D326 and D327 are the cross-instance reservation and lease rulings, D328 limits keyed-first admission to
same-key successors, D329 records what the state-write benchmark exercises, D330 records the block's verdict (the
whole formation improves in both contrasts; the end of formation is flat and SDK connect unchanged), and D331 names
the join chain as the next slice's first item.

### Formation tail 4: creation-order claims, chain heads first, the join and the connect off their round trips, then 15 → 30 → 50 hosted

Measured on main after #689 (§1.16): the hosted joins were no-ops whose cost grew with every concurrent handler; the
group-key chain sat idle 10.9 s while a claimed join waited behind WS connects; NEW claims followed the row-id
sequence's 100-id blocks, so tickets waited 14–17 s for sub-second handlers; the WS connect made about 14 read and 9
transaction round trips; the outbound worker read a commit group's observations one member at a time; and
disconnected sessions stayed 30 days under every principal scan. Decisions D339–D346, each RED first at its owner and
under the maintainer's rulings R1–R4, are judged by Postgres pins of the claim order, the join's one-batch read, the
enqueue-time ingress across a conflict retry, the connect's statement counts and the retention purge; the local 50
queue age per command type with phase timing; local 15 → 30 → 50 and the local Postgres ALM lane; the medium-scale
gate with unchanged constants; the state-write comparison; the uninstrumented hosted 15 → 30 → 50 on one head; one
frozen native-50 A-B-B-A block main against the head with timing off; one timing-on hosted 50 for the acceptance
figures (no ticket over 2 s for a sub-second handler, the claimed-join idle under 1 s, the WS connect handler p50
under 1.2 s, the join chain under 15 s); and one CPU/GC capture. D332–D338 record the surveys' facts, D347 the test
isolation rule, and D348 the follow-ups, first the outbound durable worker's flight. D349 adds the browser dialer
repair for a lane failure the slice found and traced to main: an in-flight dial to a peer that lost presence is
dropped on the offering side, judged by the dialer unit tests and the Postgres ALM lane.

### Formation tail 5: decode once, live rows only, coalesced completion polls, the outbound group's reads in two rounds and the summary worker's stall, then 15 → 30 → 50 hosted

Measured on main after #690 (§1.17): the API main thread was 92.7 % busy in the hosted 50's setup window and the WS
connect, the longest job in the flight, was bound by it; about 6 s of the connects' CPU decoded rows they dropped (about
1,200 stored sessions read per connect, about 25 used), the decoders cloned every value for nothing, each read batch was
validated twice with two `TextEncoder` buffers per key pair, and stored rows were walked for JSON safety after
`JSON.parse`; the strict groups list scanned the workspace on the SDK `connected` gate; every completion waiter ran its
own safety tick (1,416 polls in the window); the outbound worker read each row's authority in 3 + 1 sequential reads;
and the connect's commit woke nothing. Decisions D362–D374, each RED first at its owner and under the maintainer's
rulings R1–R4 (D358–D361), are judged by unit and Postgres pins of one decode, one validation per read, the containment
and expiry selectors, the strict list's set and order, one status statement per turn and the outbound read rounds; the
local 50 with phase timing against `base5-34fe4422a-50`; local 15 → 30 → 50 and the local Postgres ALM lane; the
medium-scale gate with unchanged constants; the state-write comparison; the uninstrumented hosted 15 → 30 → 50 on one
head; one frozen native-50 A-B-B-A block main against the head with timing off; one timing-on hosted 50 for the
acceptance figures (the WS connect handler p50 under 1.2 s, completion polls in the setup window under 600, connect
commit → first page settled under 20 s, `GET …/groups` under 0.4 s); and one CPU/GC capture with the thread's busy share
under 80 % and no `structuredClone` under the decoders. D350–D357 record the surveys' facts. D375–D383 come from a CI
medium-scale red the slice met and traced to main: a draining outbox pass with no lane budget let a self-feeding RETRY
lane starve the presence summary's NEW rows for 38.8 s; they are judged by the engine, Postgres and worker pins and the
medium-scale gate with outbox pass timing. D384 and D385 record the registries, D386 the follow-ups, first the outbound
worker's flight measured on a new timing-on run, D387 the lane-share ruling from a main-baseline medium-scale
comparison, and D388 the result-bytes fix from the state-write comparison.

### Formation tail 6: the outbound batch in a handful of round trips, chain heads by extension, result reads on a wake, the SQL wire and the RTT re-plan hold, then 15 → 30 → 50 hosted

Measured on main after #691 (§1.18): the durable outbound worker was the saturated stage, a 16-row batch cost about
164 sequential round trips and decision plus bundle was 58 % of the hot stretch; most completion polls were
safety-tick reads, and every wake read the status before the result; the claimed-join idle was one priority inversion
of a `<group>` run over its `<group>:aggregate` joins; fragment statements leaked an origin `Error` each and lists ran
a regex scan; keys decoded twice and audience snapshots validated four times; the sign-in probe scanned the scope; and
an RTT-only replan made the third layout. Decisions D395–D402, each RED first at its owner and under the maintainer's
rulings R1–R3 (D392–D394), are judged by Postgres pins of the bundle (3 statements in the transaction), the dispatch
(16), the decision (1 round) and the release (1 statement), unit and Postgres pins of the promotion rule, the winner,
the interned fragments, the strict list's statements, the key decoder and the RTT hold; local 15 → 30 → 50 and the
local Postgres ALM lane; the local 50 with phase timing against `base6-133df2c0d-50`; the medium-scale gate with
unchanged constants; the state-write comparison; the uninstrumented hosted 15 → 30 → 50; one frozen native-50 A-B-B-A
with both contrasts favouring the head on the whole-formation primary and on the connected-barrier wait; one timing-on
hosted 50 for (a) connect commit → first page settled under 6 s, (b) a 16-row batch under 40 round trips, (c)
completion polls under 600, (d) the claimed-join idle under 0.3 s and the last join's end before 35 s, (e) the strict
groups list under 0.4 s and (f) the first room view after a join under 3 s with no RTT-only layout within the minimum
age; and one CPU/GC capture with the API thread under 75 % busy and a flat heap after full GC. D389–D391 record the
surveys' facts, D403 and D404 the review rulings, D405–D408 the plan-cache trap the medium-scale run exposed and its
fix, D409 a harness fix, D410 the deferred items and D411 the follow-ups.

## 5. Validation

Per slice: the focused Vitest and Deno suites of the touched owners; `npx tsc` for shared,
shared-web, shared-server; `deno task check` for api-v1 and the control server;
`check:browser-bundles`; `npm run check:repo-style:changed -- origin/main HEAD`; the tests-typecheck
and coupling gates; `npm run test:full-stack:memory`; the Postgres medium-scale gate and the
state-write comparison for every server mutation-path change; the local all-local 15-agent run
before and after (D204); the Branch Release Gate, Formation and Medium-Scale hosted gates on the PR
head; a hosted 15-agent dispatch from the branch when the local run passes.

## 6. As applied

**Step 2d (PR #667).** D219 landed with `inboundVolatileStores` required through the middleware
construction and the WS server service, built by the api-v1 composition root only when relayed
ACK notices are absent (single-instance profiles; a Postgres pub/sub cluster keeps the durable path,
with an invariant that throws on the unsafe combination): 38 → 0 PGlite statements per relayed
signal. D224 found the outbox breaker charging typed `retry` outcomes (owner misses) as failures
and stopped it. D220 landed as breadth-first attach from the leader after the review found the
insertion had no depth bound (director depth 3 against a bound of 5 at 50); D221 as the
eccentricity rule (exact leader-change detection would need a root on the persisted snapshot) plus
the recipe appointing the director first; D222 as `refreshRoomState` under one deadline; D225
and D229 from the hosted 50 evidence; D226 taken and refuted; D230 after the first refinement
rebuilt the breadth-first tree; D231 after the hosted control server stopped answering its export.
D232 after the hosted control server died ≈70 s into every 50-agent run. Measured in §1.10: hosted 30 and hosted 50 pass.

**Step 2e (PR #668, head after the fix waves).** D233–D238 landed as recorded except that the second half of D238 (one RTT submission per session per refinement round) was not taken: the command, its receipt identity, the topology work payload and the recipes all name one measurement, so carrying several is a new command in all but name. D237's cap lives in the auth service (registration and the replay verify) and in the login's password verification, composed from the shared bounded concurrency admission (D246). D242 and D243 landed with the recovery lanes advertised from presence alone, the FAIRNESS selection limiter and `maxFairnessSelectionsPerWindow` removed, the pass timing observer installed once by the reader's owner, and the configuration decoder owning the host-derived derivation bound. D244, D245, D247, D248 and D249 landed as recorded, and the audit's four small primitives (failure backoff, timers, repeating run, single-flight) with thirteen consumers. Measured in §1.11; hosted 30 and 50 pass through GitHub Free.

**Step 2f (PR #669, head after the fix waves).** D250 landed with an explicit `supersededFence` per caller (`promote-dominating` for the topology publication, `reject` for activation and connect) and the publication timing line recorded after commit. D251 landed with a 1 s safety tick and, for completions handled on another server, the relay of D263. D252 needed no change: the route already made one session read and wrote nothing (the bearer lookup's second session read is the open item). D253, D254 (bounded by the earliest cached group snapshot expiry; a stamp in the browser clock's future is not trusted), D257, D258 and D259 landed (D259's counter scope is a decorator over the wrapped transaction so hooks on that handle still apply; the handler's read phase still reads the process-wide counters). D255 was deferred (socket registration must follow the connect commit) and D256 revised (no claim window on the serial outbox slot; the shared `ThrottledFlush` replaces the control server's snapshot persistence state machine). D261 landed and the engine review's findings followed as D263–D265: the cross-server completion relay, a required enqueue context with per-group system commands and a disconnect in its connect's context, and one NEW head per round over one shared eligibility predicate. D262 recorded the gate's verdict. A persisted command without a context is corruption, so commands in flight across this rollout that lack one are rejected rather than run. Measured in §1.12; hosted 30 and 50 pass through GitHub Free.

**Step 2g (PR #670).** The step began as the per-player cost on the hosted lane and became, through the D266 measurement, the hosted lane's database: D286 (ruling) moved it to Postgres with the `prod` profile, a wipe and migration on each rollout and a stopped API on a failed one. The AppInbox lane landed D267, D268 (completed replays rely on the route's bearer check), D269 (the route reads the ticket by digest before any session read or enqueue; the connect's own rows wait for the socket), D270 (a deadline below the lease from the claim), D271, D272, D277, D296 (K = 4 distinct-context heads; a lost reservation releases the rest of the round and its failures) with D301 as compute serialisation by colon-path keys, and D304 as a 100 ms cap on the conflict's backoff. The outbox and queue landed D287 (turn-batched reads through a shared primitive, one decode per row, the claimed entry kept for the guarded write), D290 (one probe per round, type-scoped wakes), D294 and D297 (dequeued rows group-commit per sender, reads two at a time). The reads landed D273 with D283 (a reversed-key suffix index replacing the namespace index), D282, D289 (group-row reads, row-shaped batch reads, a revision-probed snapshot cache whose miss is one versioned read), D290 (a removed group's work completes as a no-op) and D295. D274 and D288 bound and trigger the replan; the scale rooms are capped at the cohort. D299 sends a signal to a local current-generation recipient at once. The browser landed D284 (ruling; the generation is read per socket over HTTP and joins send the heartbeat lease), D292 and D305. The harness landed D275, D276 and D279 (ruling). D300 was reverted because a delta carries one event (§3). D285 (ruling) wipes presence and topology work rows by hand at the production deploy. Measured in §1.13; hosted 15, 30 and 50 pass uninstrumented on Postgres.

**Formation tail 3 (PR #689).** D321 landed as a standing `DequeueLaneFlight` owned by the AppInbox
`InboxQueueReader` and selected by `newLaneHeldRows`; the outbox reader, the per-pass lanes and the browser engines
keep the draining pass, and a test pins it. The slot admission gained a preferred class, and `ExclusionKeyedAdmission`
now lines up a released successor before its predecessor's slot frees; the earlier promise chain handed that slot to
a waiting keyless run, a race the RED test exposed. The engine retains each flight row's settlement, `stop()` joins
them, and each settlement wakes the engine. The pass timing attributes each row to the pass that claimed it. The
review waves added, each RED first: an external write wakes the flight while it awaits a settlement; the settlement
wait expires at the engine's idle ceiling, so due RETRY, TIMEOUT and FINALIZATION rows are probed again; FAIRNESS
skips contexts in flight and contexts whose release failed, and TIMEOUT skips contexts in flight only, since it must
reclaim a failed release; a failed release keeps its context excluded until its lease could have run out; a release
error is reported once, through the pass that rethrows it or through `stop()` when no pass is left; the flight
listener is a constructor input, and that guard found a PGlite test whose settlements went unjoined; the begin-wait
clock runs only with timing on. D322 landed as a required key with the code default removed. D323 landed as
recorded: the interleaving proof runs on both the draining lane and the production flight, and the convergence proof
(three runtimes, a lease expiry, self-leave and owner removal against a connect in both orders) runs on the draining
lane, because its hold needs a blocking pass. D324 landed for every exact-path command, with a read-phase timing
line for `readWithSnapshot` and the state-write diagnostic association extended to it; an unread snapshot now fails
loudly, and a corrupt lifecycle-policy row fails as an invariant-corruption error. D325 reverted the pool line to 5 under a pin test and kept the summary fields and `beginWaitMs`, which the
harness capture keeps. D328 is applied as `releasedKeyedRunsFirst`. `processGroupSessionCleanup` is renamed
`writeGroupSessionCleanup` in the touched-file closure. Ruled and not applied: pool 10 (D325), a store-level
cross-instance guard (D326) and a longer lease (D327); D329 is recorded. Known behaviour kept: a presence event
committed beside a concurrent join carries the previous group version, as in the cluster profile already; two
sessions of one principal may conflict once on the admission row; a lowered `maxSessionsPerMember` can be outrun once
on one server; a stale presence connect pays one discarded read. The browser facade `rallar.ts` is 266.8 → 267.9 KiB
brotli under a budget raised 267 → 268 KiB, and the headless budget 356 → 357 KiB. Measured in §1.15: hosted 15, 30
and 50 pass uninstrumented (runs 39498892622, 39499824957, 39500626461), the primary at 50 is 43.123 s against
44.653 s on #687; the A-B-B-A block verifies the whole-formation benefit in both contrasts (−12.378 and −6.344 s;
symmetric −9.361 s, −15.6 %, against a +6.024 s baseline drift), with the end of formation flat and SDK connect
unchanged (D330); the timing-on hosted 50 puts lane occupancy at 0.721 against 0.355 and names the join chain as the
next owner (D331); the state-write comparison (run 39498888877; hot +2.86 %) and the medium-scale gate (2,842 passed,
0 failed) pass. Open for the next slice, first the join chain: the join handler's cost on Postgres (0.167 → 0.310 s

**Formation tail 5 (PR #691).** Five tasks ran in parallel on disjoint files, and a sixth came from a CI red. D362 and
D363 landed with one `ValidationValue` / `ValidationRecord` owner for the client and group validators, one
`requireUnitRate`, and `validateStoredGroup` split into named parts (98 → 28 lines); the review also dropped the walk
from the stored idempotency and authority-guard reads under R4. D364 landed with the result validator renamed
`assertRuntimeStateReadBatchResult` and the topology mutation read's own second validation removed in review. D365 and
D366 landed as recorded; the current-snapshot cache probe takes the same floored rows as the read, and a cache-clock
test pins it. D367 landed with a group-key sort, because the member index answers in member-key order when one group id
prefixes another. D368 landed after review restored the equal-revision content check: an equal tuple is covered only
when its content is a duplicate. D369–D372 landed after the review moved the batch from the waiter into the shared
Postgres entries repository (D370), so batching is per instance and a corrupt row fails only its own waiter; `wait-poll`
no longer carries a batch size. D373 landed after review with one key-batch read owner and the dead port member removed;
D374 as recorded. The medium-scale gate went red on `4f336adb0` (Task 4's commit, which the diagnosis cleared):
D375–D378 record the mechanism and D379–D382 the fixes. The first fix commit, `9a47a7415`, decided the replan verdict
inside the write transaction and failed the transaction-write gate with 38 blocking findings; D383 moved it out. A
payload-bound test that timed out at 5 s on the runner (5,818 ms) now crosses the limit with the widest ids and 64
connects (`d18b642ad`). D384 and D385 record the registries for the maintainer. The browser facade `rallar.ts` is 268.7
KiB brotli under a budget raised 269 → 270 KiB (268.9 KiB on `9a47a7415` and on the final head). The final fix wave
(`d3f08929d`) split `selectRuntimeStateBatchRows` into one SQL fragment builder per selector kind (125 → 33 lines, the
statement unchanged, `PSqlFragment` typed by the values it carries) and `defaultDequeue` (69 → 37); renamed the throwing
`validate*` functions the branch introduced or extended to `assert*`; made `requireUnitRate` the one unit-rate owner
(`isUnitIntervalNumber` deleted, `-0` rejected at the command too); pinned in a composition test that every AppInbox
service of the mutation runtime shares the one entries repository (topology and rtc-rtt not reached); pinned that a
commanded origin reaches the replan successor; pinned on Postgres that an expired, unpurged head answers `head-present`
(ruled acceptable); and computes the summary state once per page (21 → 2 computations for a 10-row page; the 64-connect
page 187–199 → 167–171 ms). `10b167e1a` removes two stale reviewed-disposition rows, and the final head `71925ed4c` maps
the batched status read's rows by ordinal (D388). Measured in §1.17: the CI medium-scale gate passes on `da82e8471` and
`2f8830977`, and the local gate on `9a47a7415` passes 2,842 / 0 in 129.2 s with the longest summary claim gap 4.44 s
(red 38.83 s) and 0 corruption or payload errors; CI's gate is green on `d18b642ad`, `d3f08929d` and `10b167e1a` too,
and against main on the same machine the bounded lane share redistributes an unchanged total wait (D387); local 15/30/50
pass on `d3f08929d`; the local Postgres ALM lane passes 15/15 (34.7 min); hosted 15, 30 and 50 pass at 9.129, 15.032 and
27.061 s (#690 14.397, 27.541 and 46.463 s), with connected → accepted at 50 0.823 s against 0.514 s and a benign peer
churn after a three-layout join; the A-B-B-A block meets the rule for the whole formation (both contrasts, symmetric
−20.187 s, −39.2 %, against a +3.785 s drift), its end, SDK connect, room join and the median RTC readiness, but not RTC
readiness p95, last join → all ready or shot ACK p99; the timing-on hosted 50 meets (a) the WS connect handler at 0.695
s and (c) commit → first page at 13.796 s and misses (b) the polls at 779 against 600 and (d) the groups list at 0.447 s
against 0.4 s; the CPU/GC capture puts the API thread at 80.4 % busy (#690 92.7 %), the under-80 % target missed by 0.4
points, with `structuredClone` 0.1 % and gone from the persisted-state decoders, met; the state-write comparison raises
throughput 3–12 % on bench 1 and flagged result bytes +5.1 %, which D388 fixed (+0.4 % on bench 2). Open for the next
slice, first the three missed targets, the busy share (80.4 %) through the Postgres wire and the remaining group-state
parsing, (b) through a check of the completion notice against commit visibility and a count of the tick re-reads and (d)
through a timing line for the principal read, then the outbound worker's flight (R1) measured on a new timing-on run,
then the connect path's remaining CPU (the REST list hydration, the unfloored point read's presence rows), then the
outbox's saturation at about 19 rows/s per server (D387) and the follow-ups D386 lists.

**Formation tail 6 (PR #692).** Eight tasks ran in parallel on disjoint files. D395 landed with the fence named by
every writer of the version row after review found the reversed lock order against the control admission (a `40P01`
deadlock on Postgres), one fence per commit in every backend, and a fence that writes no row throwing. D396 landed with
the handoff and the committed policy required through the bridge and the dispatch authority. D397 landed with the
release and the hold lock sharing one observation predicate (`replace-observed-resource-inbox-entry.ts`) and one insert
row shape owned by the queuebox, and the retained pending-admission read moved to the session's `readWork`. D398 landed
after review revised R2 from any overlapping successor to an equal or extending one (D393), and the final wave took the
winner from the queue (`writeIfAbsent`) instead of comparing serialized text. D399 landed after review restored the
group-row fence on the strict list's second round, compared on `snapshotVersion`, and kept absent groups in the
snapshot cache; the census keys whole shapes. D400 landed as recorded, the connect's read assertions 3 → 2 (D403).
D401 landed with the value required at the service and no code default, and the resolver field named
`rttReplanMinimumLayoutAgeMs` apart from the queued-replan floor. D402 landed as recorded. `21f89724c` starts the
dispatch pin's engine (D409). The medium-scale run then exposed the recordset statements' cached generic plans, and
`aeed54640` plans them per execution and folds a one-element set to equality (D405–D408). Measured in §1.18: local
15/30/50 pass (48.4 s, 57.3 s and 1.1 min); the local Postgres ALM lane passes 15/15 (34.2 min); the medium-scale run
passes 2,842 steps with the APP_OUTBOX drain about 1.75× slower than FT5's run and the release at 85 ms p50, the trap;
hosted 15, 30 and 50 pass at 9.987, 19.528 and 31.213 s, longer than #691 and shorter than #690; the A-B-B-A meets the
end of formation (symmetric −0.493 s, −22.4 %), room join, RTC readiness and last join → all ready, and misses the
whole-formation primary (−2.309 / +8.533 s, symmetric +3.112 s), its loss upstream of the room join in sign-in and SDK
connect, with the main leg A2 the outlier; the timing-on hosted 50 meets (a) 5.870 s, (d) the last join's end, (e)
0.313 s and (f) 2.53 s with no RTT-only layout, misses (c) at 682 polls and (d) the idle at 0.326 s, and (b) rests on
the pins; the CPU/GC capture puts the API thread at 83.4 % busy against 75 % (busy CPU −5.3 s, the window −7.7 s), not
met, with the heap flat after full GC and the fragment leak gone, met; the state-write comparison keeps throughput flat
or higher and shows 2–4 more hot statements per attempt. <!-- rerun --> The hosted A-B-B-A and the timing-on 50 on
`aeed54640`, the medium-scale A-B-A against main and the per-shape attribution of the hot statements are pending. Open
for the next slice, first the whole-formation loss upstream of the room join (sign-in's serial login and the ticket
wait), then the busy share through the Postgres wire, the shared tick's 436 status reads, the claimed-join idle and the
medium-scale outbox drain, then the deferred items D410 lists.

**Step 2c (PR #666).** D209 landed with control hand-offs admitted through the disposed check
and the circuit breaker only (`rtc-handoff-refusal` and `rateLimited` count data refusals), and
D210 with the whole accepted adjacency on `OverlayInfo` (required, `undefined` for a bootstrap
or older overlay), a pure subtree filter at the origin, the relays and the repair, and the
review's fixes recorded as D216 (the director forwards a leader shot to nobody; a stale layout
falls back to the flood for its subtree; the dead `NotReadyException` control-send branches
removed). D211–D213 landed as decided with the review's fixes (exact revision match on the layout
identity, an unclipped last quiet window, counts instead of an order pin), D214 took the command
echoes out of the result envelopes, D215 kept the rejection-log contract after the Deno gate
pinned it, D217 took the heartbeats out of the AL admission, and D218 sized the group-request
allowance by the cohort. Measured in §1.9: local 50 passes for the first time, hosted 15 passes,
hosted 30 is bound by the hosted plane's signalling throughput (step 2d).

**Step 2b (PR #664).** D194–D198 and D201 landed with the review rulings (a readiness failure joins the
registration backoff; the breaker wakes at its real reopen instant; a handler-finalized success is
returned only for a COMPLETED entry; the committer is the single completion publisher; the claim
carries its 60 s lease and a handler that outruns it is reported, with no heartbeat; the outbox
resilience is required; the client outbox is one list of write/message pairs). D205 landed with the
fingerprint omitting the leader key when there is no leader, the sparse seed rooted at the director,
a director join rebuilding, replans idempotent, and unmeasured pairs weighed above the largest
measured RTT (average director depth 2.96/3.70/4.08 at 15/30/50, never deeper than the rooted
no-RTT tree). D206 was refuted and withdrawn. D207 and D208 landed as decided. Hosted: one user per
agent (`controller-NN`) and the explicit sign-in step; the run-manifest scope classifier accepts the
workspace-level sign-in read. Measured in §1.7 and §1.8; hosted 15 passes through the GitHub Free `hetzner-control` mode (231/231).

**Step 2a (PR #662, head after the fix waves).** D202 landed as recorded in its row (immediate
`accepted`, status-aware duplicate key, upward filter by frozen audience, volatile-lane control
ordering with D19 kept for durable work, RESERVED-indexed finalization, retained conflicting
receipts, per-message relay budget, publication failure as an observation). D199 and D200 landed
with one rollup per affecting message reused by the persistence flush, in-place trimming, compact
change-driven snapshots, an unframeable result reported as a small failed result, cursor-based
waits, a report projection only on completion, the headless app's paced render and HMR off, and
the server refusing a frame limit below the client's. D203 added the co-located per-IP limits and
the two-cause registration report. Measured: local 15 and 30 pass, 50 fails in the window (§1.6);
hosted 15 from the branch (run 38628301915, registration enabled) still fails at setup with 13
join requests and one presence update refused 503 "App inbox entry did not complete within the
wait budget" and the director timing out on RTC readiness — the serial-AppInbox burst step 2b
targets. A first hosted dispatch without `register_before_login=true` failed at the first login
(the rollout wipes the in-memory API's users) and is an operator error, not evidence.
